## 🎙 PLAYTEST FEEDBACK & BACKLOG — v0.9.8 (2026-10-18, designer voice-note; "don't change anything yet")

**Verdict: the mod is LANDING.** Balance "feels very real." Can beat e.g. Queen Slime normally-but-hard; sometimes loses WITH militia (intended — militia add HP + chaos, making it harder to read what to dodge). Soldiers feel smart/organic/intelligent, NOT OP; "not entire new players" but meaningfully influence boss fights and what you play. Player still clearly strongest (does ~40–60% of DPS) but soldiers are significant — they kill slower because their gear is UNOPTIMIZED (no reforge), and that is FUN, not a flaw. (Playing with Magic Storage, which makes the dump-into-chest loop painless.)

**PROTECT THESE (do not "fix" them):** (1) the loot loop — dump garbage, keep a varied army; an off-class boss drop (a mage weapon while you play summoner) is exciting because it upgrades a soldier instead of being junk. (2) The power gap — you're the strongest by far; soldiers slower-to-kill because unreforged. Any gear buff must stay GENTLE so it never erases this. (3) The "put in effort → stronger soldiers" fantasy (mining hundreds of ore, making super-soldiers, upgrading over time).

**BUGS / ROUGH EDGES LOGGED (designer says low priority):**
- Soldiers get stuck sometimes, but ONLY deep underground / tight tunnels; moving keeps them catching up. (pathfinding, nav in tight space)
- Occasional "run, then jump for a while" twitch. (jump-path heuristic)
- Movement-accessory VISUALS/SFX missing: hovering on rocket boots shows no particles; Hermes/Lightning-boot-type running has no dust/sound. The dummy Player's movement effects aren't drawn on the NPC. (cosmetic)
- Whips still look weird (hasn't really used summoner armor — ore armor is easier).

**DESIGN BACKLOG (all PARKED pending designer go-ahead; nothing confirmed):**
1. **Gear economy — REFRAMED (v0.9.8 clarification). The real problem is SCALING, not difficulty.** Terraria balances for one player finding ~one copy of a thing; the army needs QUANTITY. Mining scales infinitely (ore armor + repeaters win by default); THROUGHPUT-LIMITED drops (spider shards, souls, boss one-offs) can't be multiplied to 30–60 soldiers, so the classes gated behind them get structurally sidelined. **Summoner is effectively dead** past early hardmode: gated behind specific bosses AND reliant on set bonuses/minion-count buffs a soldier can't stack (e.g. Blade Staff needs Spider armor; spiders are throughput-limited, you can't farm them like ore). Army phased out summoners → mage/ranger/melee. Ranger strongest (range = huge). Mage a bit hard. Melee a bit weak (craftable ore melee < found melee like Bananarang; no range), BUT melee troops help as frontline + their sidearms "absolutely help" (Blade of Grass sidearms "kick ass"; different arrow types visibly matter — Ichor/Hallowed/Crystal). Designer is NOT asking to make specific armor cheaper — he accepts this as part of the charm — he just wishes sidelined classes were reachable. He LOVES the "limit by accessories" system (soldiers pick class armor + strongest weapon + strongest arrows; accessory choice = mobility vs damage, damage ones harder to get). CORE LOOP TO PROTECT (he re-affirmed hard): you NEVER miss out on loot — an off-class drop (Chlorophyte Crossbow, Daedalus Stormbow, Sky Fracture, a mage weapon while you play summoner) goes to the army and it looks/feels cool, "bigger than a 30% damage bonus." Found > craftable is good incentive.
   - **REFORGE (designer LOVES this; refined spec):** a **bulk "reforge-to-threshold"**, NOT pick-a-modifier-reroll-until-it-hits. You set a FLOOR tier (e.g. "at least Demonic" / "at least Rapid") and it rerolls each item only until its modifier rank meets the floor — so you can bring a whole army up to a quality bar without spending absurd money chasing Mythical on every single weapon individually. MUST BE EXPENSIVE (he insists: "not cheaper at all"). Money is explicitly NOT a constraint he minds burning — ~150 platinum spent this playthrough on recruitment drives etc., and that's GOOD; the garrison costs ~nothing, also fine. So a big money sink is desired, not a problem. **(my take: implementable — Terraria prefixes have a known value/tier ranking per item category, so "reroll until rank ≥ floor" is well-defined; and it's self-balancing — a bad-luck item costs more rerolls to floor, so cost scales naturally. This is a cleaner spec than my earlier vague "bulk reforge," and strictly better than a vendor, which would collapse the varied-army loot loop. Keep modifier effects vanilla/gentle so the player's power gap survives.)**
2. **Militia orders.** Militia follow no orders — they walk in circles and die. Should default to a sane preset (Search & Destroy / Free Rein / a defend-the-settlement doctrine), maybe barracks-settable. **(my take: clear easy win, low risk — probably the first thing to do.)**
3. **"Feel like players" MOVEMENT.** Soldiers should move/fly/dodge/dash and use double-jumps like players actually USE their mobility accessories (wings, boots, dash shields, double-jump bottles). Keep the rule: act/look like players, use weapons like players, but NOT OP like players. **(my take: biggest "alive" payoff and dead-on the North Star, but the hardest — driving flight/dash/double-jump through the dummy-Player on an NPC. The missing boot/wing particles+SFX are a smaller first slice that could ship as a taste.)**
4. **Flails & yoyos.** Still unsupported (control-scheme weapons); designer misses them (iconic). **(my take: now plausibly feasible — the Flying Knife rework proved we can drive an owner-anchored/tethered projectile ourselves. A yoyo ≈ tethered orbit, a flail ≈ thrown-and-retracted melee. Medium effort each; worth revisiting with that pattern.)**
4b. **SENTRIES (designer now wants them; previously unsupported).** His reasoning they're NOT broken on soldiers: sentries are already designed to run on players (8-player servers → ~38 sentries); and soldiers are NOT buffed like players (no armor/accessory stacking of +damage / -use-time), so you can't get thousands of hyper-buffed sentries. Mechanica adds ~dozens of sentry types → allowing sentries makes many more weapons viable. **DOVETAILS with the summoner problem (my take, real insight): sentries are the summoner content that actually SCALES to an army — they don't depend on minion-count set bonuses the way staff-summoners do, so they revive summoner flavor WITHOUT the spider-armor/set-bonus grind.** Also his request: let MELEE troops take a **sentry or summon-weapon SIDEARM** (melee soldiers sometimes carry no sidearm — dump a sentry/summon staff in and they'd use it). **GOTCHA I found in code: `ArmoryTE.IssueGear` deliberately gives a melee-primary soldier NO sidearm (`!primaryMelee` guard — "save the swords for the gunners").** So the sentry/summon-sidearm idea REQUIRES carving an exception: a melee primary still gets no blade sidearm, but MAY take a sentry OR summon-staff sidearm (so even frontline melee fields a minion/turret).
   - **CORRECTION (designer, do not lose this): sentries are ADDITIVE, not a summoner replacement. He wants SUMMONERS AS SUMMONERS — real minion-summoner soldiers must work too.** The thesis (my framing, he endorsed the spirit): a player summoner is a precision instrument (minion cap, set bonus, whip-tagging); an ARMY summoner can't be that and shouldn't try — it should be **summoner-by-VOLUME**: 20 soldiers each fielding one or two minions (cheap staff + Bewitching Table +1 minion + whatever minion slots a soldier gets) = a swarm. That mirrors the whole mod's "unoptimized but real" ethos — the player is the precision summoner, the army is the cloud. Melee troops taking a summon-staff sidearm means even the frontline has a pet out. A summoner soldier with staff + whip sidearm = minion + tag, a real loop at the individual level.
   - **PLACEMENT — designer says DON'T overthink it; mass forgives imprecision.** Keep it dumb: a melee troop in a fight drops a sentry where it STANDS (hold-the-line as it rushes forward), or on the platform ABOVE it if there is one; no babysitting, no perfect positioning. 20 of them placing "enough" = firing lines emerge from volume. Bonus: it gives their MANA a job (everyone banks max mana from stars and otherwise wastes it). So the sentry work is mostly "equip + a simple drop-where-you-stand rule + respect the sentry cap," NOT a positioning AI.
   - **BELOVED, PROTECT: station buffs (v0.9.2 StationBuffSystem).** Designer loves that placed stations near the armory permanently buff units (Bewitching Table +1 minion, campfire/heart-lantern regen, ammo box, etc.) — the "home base makes your army stronger" fantasy. The Bewitching +1 minion directly feeds the summoner-by-volume idea. Candidate to EXPAND (more stations matter / surface them) when we return here.

   - **SUMMONER = a real CHOICE (designer answered both of my questions).** Some soldiers ARE summoners (summon armor, staff primary); almost ALL soldiers can take a summon SIDEARM. The sidearm slot becomes a meaningful pick: **sword vs. staff vs. sentry.** Not everything-at-once — the choice is the point. **TOP PRIORITY: melee soldiers getting their own minion** (fills the currently-empty melee sidearm slot with a role).
   - **WHY summons are worth it even unoptimized: DoT + DEBUFF SUPPORT, not primary DPS.** Mass-produced Bee armor felt "a bit weak" and he's FINE with that — a minion's job is a little damage-over-time and (key) a debuff that helps the whole line (a debuff minion lets a melee soldier SUPPORT a fight, not carry it). Reframes summons as force-multipliers. Minion counts are also not as dire as I feared: +minion accessories are buyable/easily farmable, Bewitching Table + Summoning Potion stack, a mid-game summoner with 1 accessory slot can field ~6 minions. Even non-summoners can run ~3 late-game (bewitching + summoning potion) — exactly like a real player tossing on a few summons.
   - **WHIPS should be MORE active damage** (currently weak + "look weird"). A whip is the summoner's ACTIVE tool — real summoners whip constantly for tag/speed/damage, and flasks/imbues ride on whip strikes (more debuff support). Soldiers should whip aggressively, not stand idle while minions work. (Also still a visual-jank fix owed.)
   - **SENTRY behavior detail: periodic RE-PLACEMENT with a delay, and sometimes the SAME spot 2–3× in a row**, to mimic how a real player actually drops sentries — not place-once-and-forget. Melee drop where they stand (hold the line as they push), platform above if present. Mass + re-placement = firing lines emerge.
   - **BUFF/POTION SLOTS: the single slot is fine NOW, but make it UPGRADEABLE.** Progression on buff-slot count (e.g. hardmode → +2 slots), via a CRAFTABLE (a dome/structure) that you must intentionally stock with potions you go get → a consumption economy (another loot/production sink: mana pots, summoning pots, wrath/rage, etc.).
   - **MAGES should auto-drink MANA POTIONS when low** (they sometimes run dry) — a real-mage behavior gap. Feeds the potion-consumption economy above.

**🧭 THE GOVERNING LAW (promote this — it's the mod's soul, stated plainly at last by the designer):** *"Look at how a real player who mains that class uses their weapons, moment to moment, and make the soldier do THAT."* Everything above falls out of it: mage drinks mana pots, summoner keeps minions topped + whips to tag, ranger picks/【already does】swaps ammo, melee closes and swings (+ now a support minion). This is the North Star ("emulate real players") given per-class teeth. Use it to resolve any future "how should class X behave?" question.

**🏔 TOP FEEL PRIORITY (designer, elevated above everything else in the backlog): VERTICALITY & MOBILITY.** Units clump on ONE elevation, never take high ground, don't jump onto platforms/ledges/enemies, and — his exact words — "I've never seen one use a double jump." Ranger/mage especially sit static at the enemy's Y and plink. This is THE thing making them feel "limited in their options" rather than alive. The fix is two coupled parts: (a) positioning logic that actively VALUES high ground / platforms / ledges within reach during Engage AND SearchDestroy (perch where you get a firing angle over terrain, not at the enemy's elevation — bones exist in the v0.9.0 cover-perch `TryPerch` work; make it aggressive + vertical), and (b) actually USING mobility accessories to get there — double-jump (bottles), flight/wings, dash. The double-jump is the key visual TELL: a soldier hopping to a ledge instantly reads as "it thinks like me," like the sentry re-placement sloppiness reads as human. Highest feel-per-effort item on the board now (above mana potions). Pairs with the missing boot/wing PARTICLES+SFX (same dummy-player-visuals gap).

**SUMMONER POSITIONING — ANSWERED (designer):** summoners fight CLOSE, mixed into the scrum BESIDE melee (NOT a back rank) — whipping, carrying flasks like melee, minions swarming. Their battlefield ROLE: (1) ANTI-AIR cover (minions/summons hit flyers the melee line can't reach), (2) protect/cover the weaker units, (3) hit enemies BEHIND COVER (minions fly around terrain to reach what the ranged line can't line-of-sight), (4) extra DPS. So a summoner holds a MELEE-ish standoff, not a ranger standoff.

**EMERGENT ARMY DOCTRINE (the intended battlefield shape, and it must stay EMERGENT — role comes from what a soldier HOLDS, never from an order menu):** melee holds the line → ranger + mage shoot from behind/above → summoner covers (anti-air + protects weak + reaches behind cover). The player loves that this composition arises WITHOUT micro. Verticality makes it legible: melee low front, ranged perched above/behind = a battlefield with actual DEPTH instead of a one-Y blob.

**CONSUMPTION ECONOMY — DOWNGRADED to secondary (designer).** Don't over-build it. The simple version is the whole thing: drop buff potions in the chest, soldiers auto-use their ~3; mana potions auto-sipped when low. A real thing to spend effort supplying, but NOT a big deliberate system — keep it invisible/auto, frictionless, "dump in chest."

**PLAYER-GAP IS SAFE — KEEP IT (designer emphatic):** the player feeling strongest is structural and effectively unchangeable, so don't fear scaling the army. Your edge isn't just 7 accessories + best reforges — it's PRECISION (you aim far better), being smarter, and dash/flight/rocket-boots/+130%-damage stacking units will NEVER get. Units take 8–10 shots to kill a skeleton you two-shot. Rule stays: units get class-behavior + MODEST buffs, never the player's full stacking (matches the existing "limit by accessories" + "no reforge" design).

**🔭 ARMY-FRIENDLY CONTENT LENS (my framing, useful for deciding what to enable/buff):** the mod silently re-tiers all Terraria content by "can you get N copies?" — buy-with-farmable-currency (Old One's Army medals, town-NPC shops) and infinite-craft (ore) SCALE to an army; rare single-drops (souls-gated, spider shards) do NOT. The designer's own Old One's Army farming plan (buy weapons with medals) is him instinctively seeking army-friendly sources. Lens for future gear decisions.

**DESIGNER WORKING-STYLE NOTE (for any model continuing this):** he explicitly wants OPEN CONVERSATIONAL PROSE and real back-and-forth, not terse solution-delivery — talk through ideas, have opinions, riff, ask questions. He is a true design partner and the rambling IS where the good design comes from. Match that register.
5. **PARKED as scope-creep (designer agrees):** emplaceable turrets units man ("more a commander-class submod — turns this into commanding units, not the point"); making soldiers "more alive" (idle life/behaviors — more effort, not the point). Walled off intentionally.

Designer will keep reporting accessory issues as he finds them ("keep telling you when there's wrong things with accessories, go from there").

---

## ⭐ THE DESIGN TENET (read this first, it governs every decision)

> **The game says YES to the player's power fantasy. Difficulty comes from the enemy being overwhelming — NEVER from capping, nerfing, or denying the player their toys.**

The designer's own words: *"you see this thing and say 'oooh I could place 100 of those and it'd be OP?' and then the game says 'errr no.' We say yes."* When something is too strong, the answer is a scarier enemy or a real cost (upkeep/logistics), **not** a hard cap or diminishing returns. Multiplicative aura stacking, 90 airships, a planet-covering army, a wall-of-light particle cannon — all intentional. Let them happen. Fix *perf* and *bugs*, never the fantasy.

---

## 🛠 WORKFLOW & INFRASTRUCTURE (how work actually ships here)

- **The game is ONE file:** `title.html` (~4,700 lines, vanilla JS + Canvas2D, no build step, all art procedural via `ART`/`BART` draw-functions). `index.html` redirects to it. Serve statically, open `title.html`.
- **Delegate the coding.** Implement game changes by dispatching **Sonnet subagents in isolated git worktrees**, not by hand-editing `title.html`. Run parallel agents ONLY on **non-overlapping code regions** (partition by function/region; give each an explicit "do NOT touch" list), then `git merge` each worktree branch back. When two tasks touch the same code, SEQUENCE them. Tell every agent to **commit incrementally** (a session-limit kill with zero commits loses everything — this has happened).
- **The designer's turns are for DESIGN** — ideas, mechanics, feasibility, real opinions (not "nice, big change"). Have opinions. Be a partner. Don't narrate git/merge logistics unless asked.
- **Branch:** develop on `claude/game-balance-tweaks-x4odw9`.
- **Deploy flow (the designer plays `main`):** for each shippable change → bump the footer version + prepend an in-game `<details>` changelog entry (house style: emoji + `<b>` headers) → `git push -u origin claude/game-balance-tweaks-x4odw9` → **deploy:** `git push origin claude/game-balance-tweaks-x4odw9:main`. The designer authorized auto-deploy to main. (`main` may have a stale LOCAL ref — always `git fetch origin main` before reasoning about it.) It's a PWA; a hard refresh busts the cache after deploy.
- **A changelog entry rides with EVERY version.** Standing designer instruction.
- **Verification bar (mandatory, zero pageerrors):** headless boot check before every deploy.
  - Serve: `python3 -m http.server <PORT>` from repo dir.
  - Chromium: `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194/chrome-linux/chrome` args `--no-sandbox --use-gl=swiftshader`.
  - Playwright ESM: import from `/opt/node22/lib/node_modules/playwright/index.mjs`; run via `NODE_PATH=/opt/node22/lib/node_modules /opt/node22/bin/node script.mjs`.
  - In-page: set `localStorage.tm_seenTut='1'` AND `localStorage.tm_seenIntro='1'`. **The intro overlay plays on EVERY load** → after reload, click `.tm-skip` if present, then `[data-act="play"]`, then `waitForFunction(()=>typeof G!=='undefined' && G && mode==='game')`. Assert **zero `pageerror`** (a syntax error stops boot — this is the make-or-break check). Put test scripts in scratchpad, never the repo.

---

## 🎮 TOTAL MOBILIZATION 2 — concept + save-import (designer, 2026-08-20) ★★ its own concept doc, not just lore

**★ THE HOOK: load a TM1 endgame SAVE into TM2 and continue your civilization.** Your specific playthrough's identity (society state, research, roster, choices, scars) becomes the SEED — TM1's ending stops being a victory screen and becomes a save that MEANS something (Mass-Effect-import energy for a strategy game). **FEASIBILITY SPLIT (Claude, honest):** the IMPORT is very doable — TM1 already tracks the full end-state (the AAR reports prove it: society/research/roster/stats/choices); serialize to a JSON seed = small. TM2 AS A WHOLE GAME (interstellar total mobilization + three-body geopolitics + warp logistics) = a real project. But the import is the killer feature AND the cheap half; it retroactively makes TM1's ending matter. Scope shifts planetary→interstellar/interplanetary.
- **CORE LOOP = TOTAL MOBILIZATION OF ENERGY (designer's warp-catapult economics):** Kardashev level IS energy mastery, so TM2's "IC" is ENERGY. Warp catapults need enormous banked charge — ~1 YEAR for interstellar, near-instant in-system (the "2 miles vs 3000 miles" gradient; designer notes interstellar is probably STILL lowballed). This one constraint explains everything: why the Aggressor couldn't send a 2nd fleet (energy discipline), why early game is defensive (charging), why endgame is one catastrophic interstellar volley.
- **OPENING = MEGAPROJECT DEFENSE (ties TM1's Projects system, scaled up):** planetary SHIELD + ORBITAL RING buildable in ~6 months (less with telekinesis/superhumans "a few in-universe months away"). The war economy with no enemy becomes a CONSTRUCTION machine — total mobilization → total TERRAFORMING (Venus, orbital rings, Kardashev-climbing). Resolves the codex's machine-with-no-off-state: the thing that couldn't stop making war starts making WORLDS.

## 🌠 EXTRASOLAR AFTERMATH — additions (relativistic strike, tempo checkmate, unification irony, 2026-08-20)

- **★ THE RELATIVISTIC STRIKE THAT FAILS = THE CHECKMATE (designer beat):** both powers set aside their conflict to liquidate Earth with a RELATIVISTIC KILL VEHICLE — and it FAILS. HOW (Claude): not a shield (can't tank the KE — nothing can) — DEFLECTION AT RANGE. An RKV commits to a trajectory years out & can't course-correct at near-c; telekinetics/tractor arrays nudge it a fraction of a degree light-hours away = clean miss by millions of km ("Earth learned to catch bullets"). CONSEQUENCE (designer's instinct, Claude sharpened): the aggressors spent their ONE un-counterable weapon, Earth now knows the trick → it never works again. Every FAST decisive option is off the table; only SLOW (mass a real fleet, years) remains, on which timeline Earth out-develops them into irrelevance. Earth wins the TEMPO permanently. "Anything they can mobilize quickly enough has a high chance to fail."
- **★ EARTH HOLDS LITTLE/NO HOSTILE WILL — and that's SCARIER (designer + Claude):** jumped 0.7→1.2 without real trauma (hardly anyone died), holds all the cards. A superpower with NO GRIEVANCE can't be APPEASED — a hateful Earth wants something negotiable; an INDIFFERENT Earth wants nothing you can offer. Disinterest > wrath. Resolves the demobilization trap: no enemy → the apparatus turns to CONSTRUCTION/expansion, not conquest (feeds the TM2 terraforming loop above).
- **★ THE UNIFICATION IRONY (designer's multi-nation aside):** whole-planet sim = hundreds of nations, mixed results — but the strong (player-tier) roll into wherever a landing succeeded, delete the autofleet party, occupy with clones. So the DENIABLE PACIFICATION FORCE did the OPPOSITE of pacify: it UNIFIED humanity. A 0.7 planet became a 1.2 civ BECAUSE it was attacked (nations that mastered the tech absorbed those that didn't, under "survive together or don't"). The Aggressor accidentally built the single-voiced Earth they most feared. Every part of their plan produced its own catastrophe.

## 🌌 THE EXTRASOLAR AFTERMATH — three-body geopolitics & humanity's rise (designer worldbuilding, 2026-08-20) ★★ act-2 canon

Designer riffing (invested, "alt-history youtube" energy) on what happens AFTER the 2-min curb-stomp. Banked as act-2 canon; writing stays the designer's, Claude drafts to write against.

- **THREE-BODY PROBLEM:** AGGRESSOR (sent the autofleet — wanted Earth as REAL ESTATE for their own colonization, needed it pacified without interference) · BENEFACTOR (armed Earth with the exotic tech to bleed the Aggressor, their enemy) · EARTH (the pawn that ate the board — courted & feared by both, loyal to neither, REMEMBERS being used by both). Earth's position: the pawn didn't just promote to queen, it flipped the table and now deals.
- **WHY THE AUTOFLEET WAS BEATABLE (designer — fixes the design problem):** it was never the Aggressor's real army. A DENIABLE PACIFICATION FORCE — a drone fleet they could disavow, sent to quietly subdue Earth WITHOUT triggering open war with the Benefactor backing it. "Didn't mass fast enough" because it COULDN'T — full commitment risked the real war. So the invasion was a proxy op that went catastrophically wrong → escalation (the desperation planet-cracker) is what happens when deniability fails.
- **THE TWO REACTIONS (Claude sharpened, designer's seed):** BENEFACTOR'S HORROR is the bigger emotion — self-inflicted & existential: they accidentally created a THIRD SUPERPOWER they can't disarm (tech internalized/mass-produced), that owes them nothing and remembers being a tool. "You fear the weapon that woke up and looked at you." AGGRESSOR'S TERROR = "swinging at a bear nest," but a known quantity they can still calculate.
- **★ THE REAL TRAGEDY (Claude, designer's instinct):** once communication opens, the aliens may SUE FOR PEACE — and HUMANITY may be the one that can't accept it. The machine has no off-state (codex thesis): a total-war civ with a bottomless army & no enemy NEEDS something to point at. Peace is the one crisis you can't out-produce. Role reversal isn't "we're strong now" — it's "we became conqueror-shaped and nothing stops us, including ourselves."
- **HUMANITY'S POWER MODEL = LOGISTICS OF ANNIHILATION (Claude framing, designer's warp-catapult idea):** not a navy — a railgun pointed at the stars + mass-produced ordnance flung down it. Total mobilization scales interstellar the instant you have a WARP CATAPULT (est. 1–20 yrs to operational; designer doubts Aggressor can interfere): out-PRODUCE them and mail warheads across light-years, one-way, unstoppable. "Mongols with antimatter postage" — scarier than an armada (no fleet to intercept, just a shipping manifest). TELEKINETICS lift themselves to orbit → infantry ARE dropships → Earth SKIPS the naval arms race everyone else is stuck in.
- **ROLE-REVERSAL ULTIMATUM (designer, verbatim tone):** Earth shows up post-victory: "nice try, we're a very powerful & solid member of the international community now, 8 BILLION TONS of war reparations please — or we warp-catapult 200,000 nuclear warheads to every occupied system 😘." Chilling-cheerful. Complete role reversal.
- **COLONIZATION = THE WAR MACHINE SURVIVING ITS OWN PEACE:** Moon/Mars/Venus/all gas-giant moons in ~10 yrs on antimatter propulsion (faster w/ warp catapult). Not curiosity — the machine needs somewhere to point 2,748 idle clone battalions + an economy that won't stop. Manifest destiny as a jobs program. VENUS especially (hellworld only antimatter + zero-cost total war can crack) — terraforming with the leftover apparatus of total war = the game's aesthetic pointed at a planet.
- **NEXT CODEX PIECES OFFERED (awaiting designer pick):** (a) the first-contact ULTIMATUM in Earth's new voice; (b) an intercepted BENEFACTOR panic-transmission the night they realize what they made; (c) a clean FACTION-BIBLE skeleton for the designer to fill. Designer said they'll "draw up factions and things" — division of labor honored.

## ☄ THE ENEMY STRIKE-BACK & THE BENEFACTOR — designer lore + escalation (2026-08-20) ★★★ codex/story spine

Sparked by report_5 (a Full Invasion dissolved in 2 min, "not a single bombardier even left the exosphere"). The designer's escalation + backstory ideas — the strongest story material in the project. BANKED as spine; the WRITING stays the designer's (Claude drafts to write against). Also produced the FIRST CODEX ARTIFACT: "The Sky Did Not Land" (after-action human record — 3 recovered fragments + morning broadcast), https://claude.ai/code/artifact/fc884991-c55b-405b-b2ec-18fa54afcee9 (favicon 🛰️).

- **★ THE CARRIER PLANET-CRACKER (designer's best idea) — the planet-cracker fail-state, REFRAMED as DESPERATION.** A beaten fleet seals its carrier from the inside, turns it into a box for a few hundred pounds of antimatter, and rides it into the crust to take the planet with them — BECAUSE Earth's defense now threatens the aliens more than they ever threatened Earth. KEY: this fires ONLY when the player has curb-stomped past a threshold (the terror YOU inflict triggers the doomsday) — the game's thesis turned back on the player: you became the overwhelming force, and that is what earns the apocalypse. Replaces the vague "top-tier planet-cracker" with an EARNED, characterized trigger. (Ties the existing ROADMAP planet-cracker fail-state + the conduct-reactive/escalation hooks.)
- **★ THE BENEFACTOR TWIST — recontextualizes the WHOLE game.** A third party GAVE humanity the exotic tech (Mind-Forge, the doctrines) as a POLITICAL PAWN to bleed THEIR enemy (the autofleet). This finally answers the oldest design question: *why do you start with a full conventional arsenal + an exotic tech tree already available?* — someone handed it to you. Now the pawn has exceeded its purpose: a two-minute massacre proves Earth threatens its own patrons beyond measure. The "next war" is against the hand that fed you. HUGE: it's the ORIGIN of the player's power, made load-bearing, + a sequel/act-2 hook. Pairs with the conduct-reactive autofleet + the total-mobilization "what did you become" thesis.
- **ENEMY RESPONSE TO CATASTROPHIC LOSS (two branches, designer):** (1) RETHINK → antimatter atmospheric bombs / airburst walls for an overwhelming decisive strike; (2) DESPERATION → the carrier planet-cracker above. The fleet ADAPTS its doctrine after being beaten (feeds the adaptive-enemy identity).
- **ORBITAL INSTALLATIONS (designer):** the fleet has PERSISTENT orbital bases it strikes FROM (not just drops through) — gives "anti-orbital" permanent targets + infrastructure the player must go take. Structural home for the extended space theater (ties the Capital Fabricator / orbital-siege rework).
- **ULTRA DIFFICULTY (designer wants it): a tier that throws ~40× the threat because current Full Invasion is "an absolute nothingburger, nothing got through."** ⚠ ENGINEERING HONESTY (Claude, designer received): 40× the UNIT COUNT is the exact perf wall that lagged report_5 to 0.29x — a 40k-drone tide melts any phone. So ULTRA must be QUALITATIVE (antimatter walls, planet-cracker, orbital rail installations, capital-scale bosses — a few massive brutal threats) not literally 40× small drones. Same dread, shippable framerate. Pairs with BATTLEGROUPS: once the ENEMY is battlegroup entities, "40×" becomes cheap. This IS the enemy-rework/intensity-tier (×1/×2.5/×5/ULTRA) frontier — now the clear priority once the designer wants to build.
- **CANON confirmed:** Psi-Troopers deploy as SQUADS (3× per group, like normal infantry); prep timeline is ~7 YEARS (updated from 5). Player note observed: Command Helicopters were stacking defense grids to ~5.5× range / 10× fire rate (aura-stacking, working as intended per tenet).
- **PLAYER FRUSTRATION = DESIGN SIGNAL:** the designer WANTS to be threatened ("nothing got through", "a nothingburger"). The 85:1 curb-stomp + universal interception (v3.88.0) means the enemy is now the sole underbuilt pillar. The whole session keeps converging here: BUILD THE ENEMY.

## ✅ PERF LOOP — CLOSED at ~3.5× (designer called it, 2026-08-23): "stop here, 3.5× is enough"
Designer ended the perf loop after Pass 2. 5000-unit sim 301→86ms/tick (~3.5×), ~real-time at that outlier scale — good enough. Lever A (speed/fidelity option) and Lever B (aggregate battlegroups) were OFFERED and DECLINED for now (Lever B would cost the granular-soldier feel; not worth it for an edge case). Both remain available if the designer ever wants true 5k-smoothness later. Shipped: terrain cache (v3.89.2), separation cap + aura grid (v3.89.3), fortBonusAt grid + elevAt call-volume + airFilter memo (v3.89.4). Benchmark harness: `scratchpad/bench5k.mjs`.
Designer: keep running perf patches, benchmark at **5000 units**, iterate with Sonnet agents, check in; discuss GAMEPLAY-CHANGE fixes before doing them; realistic bar = NOT "3 seconds per second" (0.29× real-time) and/or a player speed/fidelity lever (NOT 60fps@5k — nothing sims 5k live bodies at 60 on a phone).
- **BENCHMARK (`scratchpad/bench5k.mjs`):** boot→prep→spawn 5000 `clone` + 60 `gtank`→time `updateGame(0.05)` ×20 + wrap-profile. THE STANDARD MEASURE for this loop.
- **BASELINE (post terrain-cache, 5020 units):** sim **301ms/tick (~3.3 FPS)**; render only ~16ms (NOT the bottleneck). updUnits=295. **elevAt STILL 88ms** — cache made each call cheap but it's called ~MILLIONS of times/tick (~500/unit); fix = CALL VOLUME (cache per-unit ground height once/tick). **~186ms unwrapped** = inline SEPARATION (degrades toward O(n²) on dense "wall of manpower" blobs) + movement + branching. aura/radar/acquire ~20ms.
- **LOOP MECHANIC:** agent lands → re-run bench5k → deploy if real win → profile new top cost → dispatch next agent → report milestone. Pure-perf = autonomous; gameplay-change = discuss first.
- **✅ PASS 1 SHIPPED v3.89.3 (main 9a82a08):** separation dense-bucket cap (SEP_CAP=12: O(bucket²)→O(n); dense 2000-blob 144→13.7ms, 10×) + aura/radar grid index (`G.auraBB`+`AURA_MAX_R`, exact). elevAt fell 88→28 for free (isWater call-volume collapsed by the separation cap). **5000-unit sim ~297→~108ms/tick (~2.7–3.6×)** — should move 5k from 0.29× real-time to ~real-time. 0 pageerrors; parity preserved (sim is ALREADY run-to-run chaotic via performance.now() SFX throttle + wave-spawner, pre-existing — changes are analytically no-op under normal conditions).
- **✅ PASS 2 SHIPPED v3.89.4 (main e4d3f3a):** `fortBonusAt` was a hidden O(units×buildings)/tick scan (6.6% of samples) → `G.fortBB` grid cache (~0.1%, biggest win of the pass); per-unit ground sampled once (`u._gy`/`eCur`/`u._wet`); `airFilter` closure memoized per-def. sim ~108→~86ms/tick. **TOTAL PERF EFFORT: 301→86ms/tick at 5000 units (~3.5×)** — 5k moved from 0.29× real-time ("3 seconds per second") to ~real-time (~10 FPS). 0 pageerrors, terrain exact (0px drift), units fight normally.
- **⛳ PURE-PERF FLOOR REACHED (agent-declared, verified).** Remaining 5k cost = inherent per-unit AI (movement/firing/branching ~27% of samples) + `sepU` pairwise math (~17%, already SEP_CAP-bounded, worse only in pathological 1px-packed blobs). No further SAFE (behavior-exact) win exists. Going further is GAMEPLAY-ADJACENT: (a) retune SEP_CAP lower (cheaper spread, slightly looser spacing), or (b) **LEVER B — aggregate battlegroups** (same-type masses → battalion entity; the only true path past this floor). **LOOP PAUSED pending designer decision on Lever B** (they're protective of the mass feel). Also still on the table: **LEVER A — speed/fidelity option** (deliberate slower-clock / auto-½× with a SIM-LOAD indicator; NON-gameplay, cheap, recommended regardless). explicit per-unit ground cache (`u._gy` — kill remaining elevAt 28ms) + profile & optimize the ~61ms unwrapped updUnits body (firing/lead math, squad, movement/branching, per-call closure allocs like `airFilter`). Agent instructed to declare the PURE-PERF FLOOR if the residual is irreducible per-unit AI → signals the aggregate-battlegroup lever is the only path further.
- **GAMEPLAY-CHANGE LEVERS (discussed w/ designer, awaiting decision):** **A. SPEED/FIDELITY lever (designer's idea, NOT gameplay):** at high load, deliberate slower internal clock / auto-½× with a "SIM LOAD" indicator instead of a silent stutter — ADD REGARDLESS, cheap safety net. **B. AGGREGATE BATTLEGROUPS (the only true 5k scaler, gameplay-adjacent):** same-type masses → battalion entity (pooled HP+count+one brain, cluster render); cost = units less granular (pool losses vs per-body HP); could auto-engage only at extreme counts. Designer protective of the mass FEEL → awaiting their gut on whether abstracting individual soldiers at extreme scale is acceptable. Est. pure-perf ceiling ~100ms/tick (~10 FPS / near-1× real-time); true 5k-at-speed needs Lever B.

## ✅ SHIPPED v3.89.1–.2 (2026-08-23, main 6d7ec55) — EXTINCTION confirm + TERRAIN CACHE (perf)
- **v3.89.1 EXTINCTION confirm:** `startDeploy()` gates `pendingDiff==='extinction'` behind a `#extinctConfirm` modal (designer's wording: "30× as many enemies... basically a min-maxer mode"). STAND DOWN / I ACCEPT MY FATE. `window.__extinctConfirmed` one-shot flag. Verified: gates (mode stays title), accept proceeds, other diffs unaffected.
- **v3.89.2 TERRAIN CACHE (the real perf win on the #1 cost):** `elevAt` → `elevAtRaw` (ground truth) + a 1px Float64Array `TERR_CACHE` (interp read); rebuilt locally via `pushCrater`/`pushLevel` wrappers on the 11 crater + 1 level push sites (+cap-drop). elevAt 9.5%→<0.5% of updateGame self-time; groundY ~8× faster isolated; parity exact (0px normal, 0.18px worst at a sink-edit kink); crater-invalidation 4.5× cheaper than naive, no thrash; 0 pageerrors; units sit on terrain. Caveat (harmless): sub-px interp diff butterflies discrete outcomes over ~1000 ticks — not a positioning/correctness defect, and the game is RNG-nondeterministic anyway.
- **★ NEXT PERF TARGETS (exposed by killing elevAt — data-driven queue):** the now-dominant per-unit O(n) costs are **`radarBoost`, unit-separation `sepU`, and `auraAt`** — each scans buildings/units per unit per frame. Same treatment: cache/spatial-index the aura+radar fields into a per-tick grid so per-unit lookups are O(1) (like the aura-grid idea from the interception work). This is the continuation of "make mega-armies breathe" — the full-frame win stays modest until these are done too. AWAITING designer go for the next pass.

## ✅ SHIPPED v3.89.0 (2026-08-23, main 618f0ec) — EXTINCTION tier + LOD groundwork; PERF HYPOTHESIS CORRECTED
- **EXTINCTION — DO NOT ATTEMPT** difficulty: diff 4 × fleetMul 13.5 → fleetMax ~4860 (~29.6× Full Invasion), real ground landings, 16-boss rotation (~1 / 42s). In the diff chip row with red warn styling. Boots clean, 220+ concurrent enemies, 0 pageerrors.
- **Temporal AI LOD:** HOT (on-screen OR within 700px of a foe) = full per-frame AI, UNCHANGED; COLD = expensive target-scan throttled to every 6 frames, staggered by `_id`. Behavior verified IDENTICAL for HOT units (seeded 40v40 skirmishes match baseline; a HOT unit fires the same tick). Render LOD (`_extremeLOD`: >1200 entities or zoom<0.55) trims cosmetic draw only. Every unit stays real (no cap). 
- **★ KEY FINDING — PERF HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG (correct the record):** temporal AI LOD gave only a MODEST win (~flat–10% idle, +2.7% in-combat). A CPU profile showed the real dominant per-unit costs are **(1) TERRAIN LOOKUPS `groundY`/`elevAt` per unit per frame, (2) SEPARATION, (3) general per-unit branching** — NOT target acquisition (which is cheap for plain ground units vs a moderate enemy count; matters more at EXTINCTION's 220+ enemies + for aircraft whose acquire path allocates a filter closure per call).
- **★ REAL NEXT PERF STEP (data-driven): CACHE TERRAIN-HEIGHT LOOKUPS.** groundY/elevAt are called per-unit-per-frame but terrain is NEARLY STATIC → memoize into a per-x height table (array indexed by x, rebuilt only when terrain is edited/sunk/raised). Likely the single biggest SAFE win, since every unit hits terrain every frame. Then revisit separation cost + the per-call filter-closure allocation in acquire. This is "battlegroups phase 1b" — the actual bottleneck. AWAITING designer nod (checked in rather than auto-dispatching another big agent after a modest result).

## ⛔→⚖ NO GAMEPLAY CAPS ON MASS (2026-08-20 ruling) — DELIBERATELY REVERSED to a WEIGHTED CAP (v3.98.0, 2026-08-28)

**HISTORICAL RULING (2026-08-20, superseded below — kept for the record):** Designer reconsidered and REJECTED combat-width / re-capping Shapeshifters+Clones: *"one of the best parts is having thousands of units... powerful computers can easily handle it."* Combat width & army-cap-on-off-cap-units were ruled GAMEPLAY CAPS → REJECTED. Thousands of units IS the game; any limit on how many FIGHT changes what the game is = tenet #1 violation. Perf was ruled device-dependent and an engine-only problem (LOD/battlegroups), never a gameplay change.

**★ v3.98.0 — THE RULING WAS DELIBERATELY REVERSED, NOT VIOLATED BY ACCIDENT.** What actually happened in play wasn't "a big satisfying army" — it was **thousands of essentially-free drones** (cdrone/kdrone/android/clone/shapeshifter-family units carried NO army-cap cost at all, see `isMechanical`'s old cap-exemption) spammed to ~9,000, dropping the game to ~3fps and washing every mass-death fight into a solid white explosion sheet. That isn't the power fantasy delivering scale — it's a legibility/perf EXPLOIT wearing the power fantasy's clothes: you can't see or feel 9,000 individually-worthless units the same way you feel a real combined-arms army. Per tenet #1 the fix is still never "cap/nerf/deny the toys" outright — so instead of a headcount ban, **v3.98.0 ships a WEIGHTED army cap**: EVERY unit counts against the cap now (drones, mechs, clones, ground/naval/sky capitals, superweapons — nothing is exempt any more, closing the loophole `v3.91.1`'s Shapeshifters-only fix started), but they count DIFFERENTLY — a drone squadron costs 0.35 weight, a normal line unit costs 1, a capital ship costs 4-6, a superweapon costs 5-7 (`deriveWeight()`/`d.wt`, ~L3313). The BUDGET (`armyCap()`) is raised well past its old headcount value and stays MP+Housing-driven — mass is still fully earned, just priced instead of free. Headless-tuned so a real few-hundred-unit combined-arms army (incl. capitals) comfortably affords ~half the budget, while pure drone-spam still walls out at **~1,850 drones** (was unlimited/~9,000) — perf-safe, but still unmistakably a swarm, not a headcount ban on any one unit type. Explosion glow also got a hard per-frame brightness clamp (`GLOW_SOFT_CAP`) so a mass-death event literally cannot wash the screen to white any more, independent of the cap. **Full writeup + tuned numbers: see the "WEIGHTED ARMY CAP" entry below.** The 2026-08-20 engine-only tools (LOD, battlegroups) remain valid and un-superseded as the answer to remaining perf cost — this reversal is orthogonal, about legibility/exploit-closing at the top end, not a retreat from "yes to scale."

## ⚙ BATTLEGROUPS — THE ANSWER TO MASS (designer direction, 2026-08-20) ★ eventual, deferred

**Designer's vision (verbatim gist):** *"i wanna do battlegroups eventually where squads upon squads will form unit battalions of the same type."* So the hierarchy becomes **body → squad → BATTALION (a battlegroup of same-type squads)** — a battalion is ONE entity with a count/HP pool + formation, simulated & rendered as a cluster that READS as many. This is the tenet-correct way to say YES to thousands: the player fields battalions, the engine simulates dozens of entities, not thousands of independent bodies. (Aligns with the HOI4-style battlegroup idea already banked in the feedback tracker — now the confirmed direction.)

**WHY (perf diagnosis, profiled 2026-08-20 from report_5 — a 3,791-unit game running at ~0.29x speed / "every second like 3.5s"):**
- Bottleneck is **`updUnits`** — 82ms of 85ms/tick at ~3,000 units (~28µs/unit), FLAT. Confirmed NOT v3.88.0 interception (76ms with OR without bombardment — the incoming-list early-out works), NOT auras (84.5→85ms adding 63 aura buildings), NOT enemies/proj/buildings (all <1ms). Purely the per-unit AI loop.
- SQUADS MULTIPLY IT: Clone Battalions (and other grp units) are SQUADS with multiple member bodies each — 2,748 Clone Battalions = many thousands of simulated bodies. Separation is already bucketed (not O(n²)); the cost is the sheer per-body AI.
- Render (frame/renderGame) was NOT separately measured (rAF-driven) but scales with the same body count — a co-cost on top of sim.

**ROOT ECONOMY DRIVER (why the player reaches 3,800):** excess IC with nothing to spend it on. Manpower/army-cap gates the normal army, so the player funnels a huge IC economy into OFF-CAP units (Shapeshifters in report_3/4, Clone Battalions ×2748 in report_5) — the only way to convert banked IC into force. Battlegroups help; a deeper IC-sink pass (more toys to spend on — tenet-positive, NOT a cap) is the complementary design lever. (Designer standing rule: do NOT change manpower — "it's perfect where it is.")

**STATUS: DEFERRED** (designer: "we can fix this later, this is very much an outlier case"). No optimization pass now. Build battlegroups as its own architectural project when picked up: battalion entity (type + squad list + pooled HP/count + formation), aggregate AI/targeting/movement at the battalion level, cluster rendering + LOD, and a UI to raise/command battalions. Reuse the existing squad system as the building block.

## 🏭 GARRISON REWORK — CORRECTED SPEC (I built the wrong thing; designer re-specced 2026-08-17)

**⚠ WHAT I GOT WRONG (v3.23 + v3.50):** I built Garrison Stockpiles as an ABSTRACT WEIGHT-BUDGET CEILING (`garrisonCap` — a number), and garrison producers/factories that SPEED the fill RATE. Designer's verdict: garrison factories are "basically useless" (speeding a free trickle isn't a decision), and stockpiles get "put deep underground and never touched" (pure ceiling → no reason to place them anywhere).

**✅ WHAT THE DESIGNER ACTUALLY WANTS (verbatim gist):** *"it's just: build garrison factories WITH UPKEEP, and they automatically fill nearby garrisons, THEN they fill storages. They have a LOT of upkeep to prevent them from being (EASILY) spammable."* So:
- **Garrison Factories** (heavy upkeep) run automatically: (1) fill nearby buildings' standing garrisons first, (2) once those are full, OVERFLOW into STORAGES (stockpiles) — banking a real reserve of actual units, not setting an abstract cap.
- **Storages/Stockpiles** become a physical RESERVE that FILLS UP (from factory overflow) and DRAWS DOWN (refilling garrisons under attack) — not a weight ceiling.
- **The anti-spam lever is UPKEEP on the factories, NOT a weight cap.** Pure tenet-#1 (a real recurring COST, not a hard ceiling). High upkeep = you can't trivially carpet the map with them.
- **NOT a "crazy supply line"** — designer explicitly rejected the deep supply-chain framing (and disliked throughput grind in the top-down rebuild). Keep it simple: factory→garrison→storage, gated by upkeep.
- Likely retire/repurpose: the `garrisonCap` weight-budget ceiling, the `garrSpd` fill-rate auras (Garrison Factory aura, Mobilization Hub), and reconcile with the v3.50 `garrWorks` localized producers (which already auto-fill nearby — keep that skeleton, swap the WEIGHT-BUDGET ceiling for the UPKEEP lever + storage-overflow reserve).
- **STATUS: designed + queued as its OWN code lane** (don't tangle with research/art lanes). Build after the current promo + research + art batch.

**✅ SHIPPED v3.100.1 (2026-09-03, main 8227929) — GUIDED-MODE DEADLOCK HOTFIX.** Action steps toggle `tut-action` on `#tutOverlay` (`pointer-events:none`, dim .62→.18, `.tut-card` stays `auto`); `render()` unpauses when an action step opens while paused; `onKey` returns early on action steps (Esc cancels placement again); `tutCountB` now also counts `G.sites` (placeBuilding pushes to `G.sites`, not `G.buildings` — the under-construction list). Verified headless: read-only welcome card still blocks + paused; power-plant step click-through (elementFromPoint → card / canvas), unpaused, placement advances the step within 1s, Esc keeps the overlay, Space toggles; 0 pageerrors. Verify script `scratchpad/v3100_1.mjs` (note: needs a free x — scans offsets).

**🧮 THE OPTIMUM-BUILD STUDY (2026-09-16) — numbers, paper build, and a 3-pass headless TAS bot (scratchpad `tas/`, no game changes).** Designer: "what is the most mathematically optimal way to play?… look at the numbers, the units, etc and find the optimum path/build." **Economy facts:** Power Plant 120 IC → +7 IC/s (17s payback); Extractor 90 → +4 IC/s; Housing 100 IC/26 MP → +3 MP/s +4 cap; every building costs `ic×.035+2` influence; influence base 2/s × stab/100 × momentum (τ25 → 3×, reset by ANY spend incl. every research draw tick); Fusion 176 inf → IC ×1.45 AND +5 inf/s (the best purchase in the game); Space Age 448 → IC ×1.70; Research Lab 170 IC → −8% cost / +14% speed EACH (8 labs = 36% cost floor, 2.1× speed — the single strongest influence lever, the designer's point); Comms + 2 Propaganda 410 IC → stability 65→100 = influence ×1.54. **Units (DPS per 1,000 IC):** kdrone 715, superhuman 571, shock 362, clone 293, AA 244, cruiser 208, destroyer 176, Falcon 113, infantry 111, Nanoforge 95, capitals < 70. **Free garrison beats all of it:** Barracks 70 IC → 3 inf (476/kIC), Factory 160 → tank/inf/aa (~450), Airbase 180 → 3 Falcons (506), off the army cap, 0.12 IC/s per weight, Stockpile +20 weight per 140 IC. **Anti-orbit per kIC:** orbital strike fighter 107 (needs Space Station), battlecruiser 68 (water), Railgun Lance 54 (Steel only), sats 43. **The binding constraint is MANPOWER, never IC** — confirmed three ways: the designer's report_12 (peak upkeep 638 IC/s > income 510), and the bot. **TAS bot (Sonnet, `tas/bot.mjs`, real API: `placeBuilding`→`G.sites`, `queueUnit` gated by `prodListFor`, `startResearch` draw-down, `b.autoStrike`, `u.rtb` grounding), three doctrines on one opening, General / Full Invasion, all three LOST every pass:** pass 1 — IC-maximising economy hit 657–1,235 IC/s (designer's best real match: 510) and banked 35k–173k IC it could not spend: MP sat at the 900 cap for 210s then at 2–6; 2,100+ interceptor/shield placements failed on MP; no AMI, no shields, 7 cities gone in 57–80s. Pass 2 — an "MP never idles" valve filled the army cap with 500 infantry/AA before any signature unit could queue; every build became the same blob; worse. Pass 3 — mandatory defense first (interceptor ×3, shield ×2, SAM ×2 per city), signature units second, housing when the cap blocks, IC surplus → capitals/allies: C SPACE (94 Lances + 51 laser sats + 32 AMI + 39 shields) survived 142s, 493 kills / 428 losses (ratio 1.15, first city at 75s); A GARRISON (36 Lances, 24 battlecruisers) 111s, 267/416; B NAVY (6 carriers, 25 destroyers) 74s, 113/301 — ships take 16–36s each and a 300s post-Space-Age window is not enough. Research pace was the bot's real ceiling: Fusion 86s, Space Age 153s, Automation 442s — it never reached Robotics/Colossus/Microrockets, because one-at-a-time bank-then-burst is slow and it under-built labs; the designer reaches 13 doctrines with 8 labs. **VERDICT:** the optimum is a SCHEDULE, not a doctrine, and its spine is: Plant+Housing at t=0 → bank → Fusion at ~55s → Comms+2 Propaganda → 4 Extractors (Steel first) → labs to 6–8 EARLY (each one is worth more influence than it costs IC) → Space Age → Point Defense → Shield Tech → Microrockets → production buildings + stockpiles for the free army → interceptors ×3 / shields ×2 / SAM ×2 / microbatteries ×2 per city → Lances from every factory from t≈300 → capital hulls and allies as the IC sink once MP is the wall → aircraft grounded for the first 30s → auto-silos on. That is, to within ordering, the report_12 build — a human with 13 doctrines beats every bot doctrine with 8. No "clearly defined meta": the navy path is bottlenecked by build time, the space path by the Space Station, the ground path by manpower, and the fleet's dominance read punishes any single answer — exactly the designer's intent.

**🎓 THE DESIGNER'S DOCTRINE (how they actually win, 2026-09-16) — read this before any future "optimal build" work.** Verbatim: "your time to bank influence is a time to make units since they don't need influence. buildings, research, allies, basically everything except unit production uses influence. so unit produce a lot in downtime." · "you should never sit at an empty cap and if you find yourself running out of ic or mp, build structures to raise that speed" · "spend what you have extra on the stuff you don't have enough on and fill the gap between influence jumps by creating units constantly" · "build a few factories around the map to let you start preparing rough screens, and create some fighter aircraft. That pushes more ic and MP income to build" · "if you go for upkeep heavy units, you produce a ton of banked IC to then spam them out late game before the upkeep is a problem" · "big units are multirole (a skyfortress does air and ground attack) and the altitude puzzle is very real. Get free upgrades (plasma is a really solid one)" · "one upgrade you should always take is a rotary cannon factory to turn the cities into air to air defense, you should never focus on one type of launcher anyway. layer your aa with CIWS tanks, sam tanks, aegis, infantry to build forts, helicopters, aircraft, ships" · "relying on dumb unguided weapons isn't perfect since now units can likely dodge high speed projectiles, so missiles perform more work" · **the three goals:** "prevent total dominance from space · have enough to fill a screen that means nobody will be able to land a significant force · prevent mass death via bunker buster strikes (shields, large airforces, anti missile interceptors, space units can all help)" · cheese is valid: "90 billion munition factories and planting airbases and naval dockyards everywhere to create 80 thousand fighters". **Reconciliation with the bot study:** the bot treated the influence-banking window as "do nothing" and always met the invasion with a full treasury and a small army; the designer treats it as the production window. That single rule explains the gap between three bot losses and two human flawless wins. It is a conversation, not a task — nothing to build.

**🤖 TAS RECURSION, PASSES 4–9 (2026-09-16, designer: "if they lose, keep rerunning them. i want you to recursively improve until we secure a win.") — all General / Full Invasion, diff 1.4, real economy, still NO WIN.** Pass 4 — the designer's doctrine as a bot (produce during the bank window): 90–105s, the early army was fodder for the opening barrage. Pass 5 — shields first, labs first: 7 shields up, but a labs-before-Fusion gate deadlocked the bank and Fusion slipped to 243s. Pass 6 — phased economy (Fusion 86s, full ladder): the best round ZEROED the 491-strong fleet TWICE and still lost — the win check needs `G.fleet<=0 && G.enemies.length===0`, and 150–220 landed enemies bled stability to 0 first (CLAUDE.md flaw #7 reproduced by a bot: "wow this is a great defense, oops, you lose"). Pass 7 — strike-stability gate (arm ≥75 / disarm <60, ≤4 auto silos, 1 per city) works; two placement bugs (unbounded cursor, map-wide searches defeating per-city caps). Pass 8 — seeded map (seed 2, 7 cities) + city-local placement: ladder done by 366s, stability 100 into combat, fleet 491→277, 405 buildings lost. Pass 9 — building caps + army-cap fill: utilization plateaued at 55–68% because `armyCap` moves with BANKED manpower (spending MP on units lowers the cap you were filling), best round 7 = 78 Lances / 39 androids / 222 infantry / 11 battlecruisers, fleet 491→285, stab 100 through t=510, loss at 583s. **Pass 10 — closest yet, no win.** Dropping the utilization-% gate for "never sit at an empty queue" + housing whenever headroom <10 took cap use from 55–68% to 96–102% at once (the cap-vs-banked-MP diagnosis confirmed). Reordering the ladder to reach Colossus earlier changed NOTHING — Colossus completed at the identical timestamp on two different orders, i.e. research is gated by influence pace, not tree position. Seed matters enormously: seed 3 (7 cities, more land near cities) gave a 0.91 kill/loss ratio vs 0.63 on seed 2 and a stability-starved 73 kills on seed 4. Best = round 8 (seed 3, ciws/samsite back to 1/city): 790 kills / 638 losses, 197s of combat, fleet 491→69 (86% destroyed) — then stability hit 0.2 at t=570 and never recovered; the capitulation crisis ended it at t=662 with 231–287 landed enemies still on the field. The endgame field-clear never triggered (fleet never < 60 in time). **Pass 11 — no win; LOOP STOPPED by the designer ("you can stop now, you're not going to win. i've very much identified the issue").** Stability bleed read from the code: raw `G.stab` takes one-shot hits (city death −12, Housing destroyed −5, fallout building −6, each strike its cost, floored at 0, never blocked); Comms Towers bank a `stabRes` 7-point reserve that tops stability back up but LATCHES empty once drained and recharges at only .15/s; `stabCap` = 65 + living Comms/Propaganda/Radar − dystopia − dangerous research − 3/superhuman; `cityStabPenalty` (2.5/dead district, 6/occupied city) only drags `effStab`; regen toward cap ≤ +.5/s. So once the reserve is spent, sustained combat is a one-way ratchet. Stability buildings FIRST (before defence) took fleet destruction 44% → 67%; + aegis 2/city → round 3 = 76% (fleet 491→118, 736 kills), the best of the whole series; raising the strike gate, endgame silo-all, uncapped allies and 4 interceptors/city all regressed. **Harness finding:** the page's own `requestAnimationFrame` loop was still calling `updateGame` alongside the bot's scripted ticks, so passes 8–11 were double-driven and NOT seed-reproducible (an exact replay of round 3 gave 8% instead of 76%) — treat every A/B conclusion from passes 8–11 as noisy; fix = `G.paused=true` after boot or stub RAF. **Standing design findings from the whole bot series:** (1) army cap counts BANKED manpower, so spending shrinks the cap you were filling (dropping the utilization target for "never sit at an empty queue" went from 60% to 100% cap use); (2) fleet at zero with 150–280 landed stragglers is a stability-crisis loss in practice (flaw #7); (3) research completion is gated by influence pace, not tree order (Colossus finished at the identical timestamp on two different ladders); (4) seed/map matters enormously (0.63 vs 0.91 kill/loss on identical logic). **Design findings for the designer, independent of the bot winning:** (1) the army cap's dependence on banked MP punishes spending — the exact opposite of "never sit at an empty cap" (tenet #1 candidate: cap should count MP *ever earned* or housing only, not the bank); (2) zeroing the fleet with enemies still on the ground is a loss in practice — the field never drains fast enough against the stability bleed (flaw #7 fix: fleet 0 should be a win, or a "MOPPING UP" state with the bleed halted); (3) bots never use strikes in real numbers either — 24 ballistic in the best round, matching the designer's 0 in report_12.

**✅ SHIPPED v4.4.0 (2026-09-16) — THE WALL COMES DOWN.** report_13 (General / Full Invasion, v4.3.0): 1,321 recruited / 1,319 lost, 7/7 cities destroyed in 98s of combat, damage dealt 27k vs 150k taken (report_12 on v4.2.1: 213k vs 22k), 641 aircraft, 0 shields, 7 AMI, 0 capitals. Designer: "the sheer speed of the enemy assaults caught me off guard… the bombardier fleet turned the surface into ash… the main gun erased most of my ground units… my screen was severely weakened by the cluster bombs… my entire fleet of ships firing I thought would be enough to hit into orbit but the orbital fighters were running circles around me… nerf the bombardier self defense systems significantly… this is exactly the type of rapid domination assault I wanted, but make it less completely unstoppable." **Root cause (audit):** the wall of five deleted ≈200 interceptable player rounds/s (5 bombarders × 6 per .34s + 20 escorts × 2 per .35s over dual-centre bubbles; every missile/plasball/tracer/microrkt/amslug round died to one lance — Aegis, AMI, missile sats, Sky Cruiser included) while its 40 railsats + 40 lasersats + 5 menders + escorts parked in the exosphere where v4.3's ceilings let nothing but rails/particle/space touch them; the lance was 300·invT on the first hull + a FULL-damage r90 ground burst on a miss; Zeus rods were barred from parked hulls by `notSpaceStationed`. **Built (three Sonnet builders A/C/D + coordinator tunes):** `PD_SAT` per-hull interception budgets (bombarder 5/s max 10, carrier 14/s max 28, escortfighter 1/s max 2 and self-only r220, annihilator 3/s; `pdSweep` lances ≤ floor(budget); `FLAK_CAP 12`; red flicker ring while budget < 1) · telegraphed lance (`LANCE_CHARGE_T 1.2`, `G.teleLines` dashed hostile line + dot, `SFX.charge`; `AMLANCE_DMG 220·invT`, `AMLANCE_PIERCE_HITS 2`, miss burst pays `AMLANCE_SPLASH`; coordinator: `densestShipClusterNear` skips air, the bolt passes through non-capital aircraft, `AMLANCE_SPLASH_R 60`) · bombpod bomblets 22·invT→13·invT r70; opening carpet 9·diff→6·diff · grid descends: railsat/lasersat/mender + escort fighters hold `MESO_TOP()+40+rand·80`, lasersat `airOnly`, hulls unchanged; mender 22→12 hp/s + pulse; `notSpaceStationed` allows `anchored && boss` (Zeus vs parked hulls) · **explosion families** `BOOM_STYLES` (13: he/plasma/kinetic/antimatter/nuke/fire/cluster/seismic/melt/emp/vacuum/water/collapse, each with palette + family-specific flash `fl{col,col2,mul,life,bloomMul…}` + 2–3 variants; `boom(x,y,p,style)`, `boomStyleFor(p)`, `resolveStyleAt` → vacuum above THERMO_Y / water on the sea; new particle kinds shard/arc/plume/spray/wavefront; `groundBurst(x,power,style)` terrain-aware clod tint by `elevAt`, spray on water, wavefront + crater for p≥12; `pickStyle` extended) · **ally landings** `allyLandingFx` + `G._allyDropQ` drained one per .12s, one `⚑ ALLIES LANDING ×N` per delivery (spawn stays immediate, only FX are staggered) · **Warthog** `weapon2:'rotary' 26/.6/340 ceil2 .78 heavyGun` (p.heavy tracers 2× width, spark + small kinetic boom on ground), **Mastodon** `weapon2:'tracer' 18/.35/300` · **coordinator: side-aware wreck shrapnel** (`shrapnel(x,y,n,dmg,side)`; your wrecks hurt only enemies, theirs only you, strikes hurt everyone — shrapnel was ~1/3 of all wall-bench losses, a hidden anti-mass tax). **Bench (scratchpad `v44kills.mjs`, 1080p world, 1,058-unit report_13-shaped army, no buildings, deterministic RNG):** wall of five, 60s — v4.3.0: 1,058 lost (all, by 55s), grid 40/39/5/24 alive, bombarders untouched, 1.9k dmg dealt; v4.4.0: 321 lost, grid 0/0/0/1, two bombarders dented, 8.3k dealt, 280 of the 321 kills = the lance on a 40px-packed ground blob. Full opening phases 1–3, 110s — v4.3.0: 1,058 lost by t=100, 12k dealt; v4.4.0: 769 lost, 40.7k dealt vs 75k taken, top killer = bomblets (313, unshielded infantry/AA/SAM packed on land), then rails, lance (naval 106 / ground 73), busters. 0 pageerrors on every run. **Not done / flagged:** `bombarderGroundSnipe` has no range limit (out of scope, candidate for a pass); the 400-bomblet carpet is still the top killer of an unshielded ground screen (designer's shield doctrine counters it; watch the next report); TEMPORAL AI LOD cold-freezes a lone unit's re-acquire when nothing is nearby (surfaced by a 1v1 bench, not real play); the designer's manual text on bombarders was left as is (still true). **Projects: BANKED** (see the memo section).

**✅ SHIPPED v4.3.0 (2026-09-10) — EVERY GUN HAS A CEILING.** Designer, after the report_12 analysis: "a lot of units seem to be able to hit into space… units that fire into the air should either weaken or fizzle out entirely over time… without gravity calculations… units that aren't supposed to hit space just have their projectiles keep traveling" → "stratosphere is way way too low, at least for plasma guns. plasma is supposed to hit mesosphere… check what each ship has in terms of weapons… orbit (the biggest autofleet strength in canon) is getting wrecked [because] plasma rounds keep going way after they should have died. bombardment should be a thing you need special units for" + "remove the heavy alloy requirement for railgun tanks." **Audit finding:** the acquisition ceiling defaulted ground .80 / air .56 / **naval 0 = unlimited** — every gun hull on the sea (destroyer/carrier/supercarrier tracer, cruiser rotary, battleship rockets) could target orbit; sky-ship plasma (`skydestroyer/skyrailgun/nanoforge`) sat at `.7` (stratosphere); no projectile carried a ceiling — shells (life 9s), plasma balls (4s), tracers, rockets homing on a climbing target all kept flying past the shooter's reach and hit whatever was above. In report_12, 105 naval hulls were shooting the flagship group from the sea. **Design:** `weaponCeil(d, wkey)` — explicit `ceil` wins, else by WEAPON CLASS: guns (tracer/rotary/shell) .58 stratosphere · plasma (plasball/plasarc) .40 mesosphere · unit missiles (rocket/missile/microrkt) .40 · lasers (beam) .40 · psionic plasma .40 · rail/rod/particle/amslug/aegismsl/space units/strikes 0 unlimited ("bombardment needs special units"); sky-ship plasma `.7 → .40`; every other explicit value untouched. Rounds carry `p.ceilY` via `FIRE_CTX` set by the shooter; above the ceiling damage fades over `CEIL_FADE_PX = 120` then the round fizzles (two comparisons per projectile per frame, no gravity); homing rounds drop lock on a target above their ceiling; beams and splash honour it; enemy rounds unaffected. Railgun Lance `comp:{alloy:20}` deleted. One Sonnet agent. **BAND CONSTANTS CORRECTION (important):** the live code is `SPACE_Y .22 · THERMO_Y .56 · MESO_TOP .62 · MESO_Y .67 · STRATO_Y .76 · TROPO_Y .82` (smaller = higher) — NOT the 12/40/58/74 that CLAUDE.md and my plan carried; the builder flagged the drift and I corrected `weaponCeil` before shipping: guns `.67` (top of the stratosphere), plasma/missiles/lasers/psionic `.62` (top of the mesosphere), unlimited 0; sky-ship plasma `.7 → .62`; naval default `.67`; air default `.62`; the Missile Cruiser's Aegis rounds `ceil2 0 → .62` (16 cruisers were plinking orbit with anti-missile rounds); CLAUDE.md band line fixed. Built: `weaponCeil` also feeds `defaultCeil` (squad bodies, readiness `tutBestCeil`, building tooltips), building plasma-upgrade path `min(.62, base)`; `FIRE_CTX`/`fireCeil()` at every player fire site (units, turrets, squads, buildings, AMI, city defence), `p.ceilY` stamped in `fire()`, `CEIL_FADE_PX 120` fade/fizzle in `updProj`, `p.fade` honoured by the hit loop / `plasmaBurst` / `splashDamage`, homing lock drop + `near(...)` ceiling filter, `beamHit` ceiling param. Verified (builder, 0 pageerrors): fade 0.5 at 60px over measured end-to-end 0.499; destroyer never fires at a space bombarder, battlecruiser deals 1281 in 30s; skydestroyer hits at .45 not .30 (old fractions) — re-verified by me on the corrected constants far from cities (`scratchpad/v43b.mjs`): skydestroyer hits .66 / nothing at .45, destroyer hits .72 / nothing at .45, Falcon rocket hits .66 / fizzles at .45, battlecruiser rail reaches .30. Perf: `v3103b` 2.8 ms/tick merged (was 4.1–4.8 in 4.1) — the builder's live-invasion A/B was too noisy to call within 5% and said so; isolated function timings showed no regression. Bench R11 shape: loses at ~135s (was ~180s on 4.2) — the naval fleet can no longer kill the orbital layer, so the flagship group grinds; that is the designed effect ("orbit is the autofleet's strength… bombardment needs special units"). Regression: tutquick, v41, v403, v401 green.

**✅ SHIPPED v4.2.1 (2026-09-10) — THE WALL OF FIVE.** report_12 (General / Full Invasion, prep 7:30, combat 3:27): **FLAWLESS VICTORY** against the 4.2 fleet — 5/5 cities, 0 districts lost, 783 recruited / 183 lost, 742 kills, 26 superweapons incl. the flagship + 13 bombarders, 13 doctrines to plasma/space, a GROUND doctrine (150 plasma infantry, 106 androids, 14 Land Dreadnoughts, 13 Colossi, 8 War Colossi, 6 Mobile Fortresses, 20 Shield Generators, 15 AMI, 15 microrocket batteries); air punished as asked (85/130 Falcons, 35/47 Wardens lost); upkeep 177,861 of 189,012 IC (94%, peak 638 IC/s); 0 strikes for the second match running. Verdict: the Orbital Fortress is beatable by a real build, numbers untouched. Designer: "when i said bring back bombadiers i meant bring back the wave of 5 spread out evenly across the map, with their defensive satellites" → `ESC.OPEN_BOMBARDERS 2 → 5`, used for invasion/glass/extinction alike in `phase3AllOut`, manual Phase 3 sentence. Verified (`scratchpad/v421.mjs`): 5 bombarders within 1s of the phase-3 banner, spread 85% of the map, 4 escorts each, all 5 grids deployed (80 sats + 5 menders), 0.7 ms/update in that window, tutquick/v41/v403 green, 0 pageerrors. **Open for the designer:** zero strikes in two matches — silos may not be worth the influence any more; upkeep at 94% of income with a dreadnought army.

**✅ SHIPPED v4.2.0 (2026-09-08) — THE ORBITAL FORTRESS.** report_11: General / Full Invasion, prep 7:30, combat 2:56, VICTORY, 3/4 cities (1 captured, never retaken), 833 recruited / 305 lost, 757 kills, 26 superweapons incl. the flagship ×1, bombarders ×6, hives ×8, leviathans ×11; peak simultaneous hostiles only 80 (killed in the re-entry window by 229 Falcons + 69 Kites); upkeep 80,920 of 95,352 IC earned (peak 305 IC/s — 77 capital hulls + 6 Nanoforges); 0 strikes; 7 doctrines. Designer: **"About right."** Difficulty, allies, fleet mass stay. New thread, in their words: bombardiers "barely try to intercept the enemy missiles" (cause: every enemy PD scan hits ONE round per .34s — nothing in the fleet can block a volley); the flagship "died way way too easy. it canonically has dozens of anti missile batteries, ciws cannons, antimatter bombs and engines… sits around a gigantic friendly fleet, massive satellite networks, constantly pumping out more and more units… they need more unit spam… a way to block a lot of projectiles flying at once"; "the nanoforge ships really need a health nerf. enough that a bombadier can three shot them with their primary guns. bombadiers also need bigger heavier guns… massive explosive lances like antimatter railguns at enemy ships at incredible speed"; "friendly satellites should not move. strike fighters and frigates exist for that reason. give the space strike fighters the fighter ai"; "where were the enemy 300 micromissile salvos during the initial barrage wrecking my air superiority… forcing them to land"; "why would electromagnetic interference happen AFTER the two strikes? make it happen all at the start." **EMP-TIMING REVERSAL (designer's call, logged):** 4.0.3 said EMP "when the biggest attack wave is attacking the main defenses" (phase 3); 4.2 moves it to invasion t≈1s. Build: A `pdSweep(e,r,nMax)` + `pdfan` (bombarder 6/tick, carrier 16, escort 2, annihilator 4) + carrier antimatter FLAK FIELD (r 260, 60 to rounds, 40·t to aircraft, every 4s); B carrier 9000·t hp / armor 30 / shield 3000 regen, 8 escorts, 10+10+2 grid, arrives with 2 bombarders + 6 pickets + 4 shield carriers, 5s producer (escort×2, raider×3, strikewing, shieldgen, interceptor×2), `ambomb` every 30s (6 × 60·t r140 + 400px blackout 12s), engines toward `densestEnemySpaceX()` under 50%; C `amlance` (3000 px/s, 300·invT, r90, pierces 300px, ship-weighted `densestShipClusterNear`), Nanoforge 3200→2400 (3 lances from ~62s); D `sat:true` station-keeping for satlaser/missilesat/rods, `ostrikefighter` fighter AI in the space band, frigate unchanged; E EMP warheads in `phase1Screening` at t≈1s, `AA_MICRO_TOTAL = 300·diff/1.4` anti-air micromissiles over 30s above `densestAirX()` bins, `airExposed` readiness reason. Three Sonnet agents (A+B / C+D / E), disjoint regions — all merged clean. **Built:** `pdSweep(e,r,nMax,cx2,cy2)` + `pdfan`; carrier `hp 9000·t / armor 30 / shield 3000·min(2,t)` regen 60/s after 5s quiet (`e.lastHitT` stamped in `hurt()`), `ESCORT_TABLE.carrier` 8, grid 10+10+2, arrival brings 2 bombarders + 6 pickets + 4 shield carriers, 5s producer (9-kind cycle, gate 600), `ambomb` (6 × 60·t r140 + `G.blackout` 400px/12s), `flagshipFlakBurst` (`FLAK_DMG 60`, `FLAK_R 260`, `flakring`), engines toward `densestEnemySpaceX()`; `amlance` (3000 px/s, `300·invT`, `densestShipClusterNear` capital ×3 / naval ×1.5, aimed at hull altitude), Nanoforge 2400 (3-lance kill verified: 1584→704→−136); `sat:true` station-keeping (x drift 0, y ±4), `fighterAI:true` on `ostrikefighter` (closes to <1% of range in 9s, returns to station in 15s); EMP warheads in `phase1Screening` at t<2 with the banner first; `G.aaSalvo`/`tickAASalvo` 48–50 carriers / 288–300 subs over 0–30s above `densestAirX()` bins (a 40-fighter squadron with no ground AA: 82% alive at 40s before → 0% after); `airExposed` readiness on `tutGroundAADps()`. **My three tunes after the bench (the lance was a base-eraser, not a ship-killer):** (1) `AMLANCE_PIERCE_HITS = 4` — the first hull takes the full lance, hulls on the pierce path take `AMLANCE_SPLASH = .3`, the bolt spends itself after 4 (a full hit on every hull along 300px was 30 stacked buildings per shot, 850 buildings in 15s); (2) the lance burst is UNITS-ONLY (the shared `splashDamage` half-damage-to-buildings still deleted ~15 batteries per shot); (3) `FLAK_AIR = 8·invT` (40 was a one-burst no-fly zone: 95 fighters → 12 in 15s). **Bench (land-only, `refdef.mjs` tiers 3 = report_9 shape, 4 = report_11 shape: 50 Falcons + sky ships + naval + sats per city):** 4.1.3 R11 survives 300s (fleet 0 at 225s) — the "about right" match reproduced; 4.2.0 R11 loses at ~180s, air 374 → 118 by 120s (the 300 AA micromissiles + flak + 30 escort fighters, i.e. exactly the air-superiority punishment asked for; the bench cannot land aircraft, build AA, or strike), buildings hold (170 → 135 at 120s) after the tunes; 4.2.0 R9 loses at ~255s (dense Goliath park under lance spall). Regression: tutquick, v41 (updated for `amlance`, 8 escorts, 20 sats, double `hurt` to kill through the shield), v403, v401, v3102 clean, 0 pageerrors. **For the designer:** this is a harder game than the one they called "about right" — by their own asks. If the next report says the flagship group is a wall, the honest levers are the carrier producer cadence (5s), `FLAK_AIR`, and `AA_MICRO_TOTAL`, not the fleet size.

**✅ SHIPPED v4.1.3 (2026-09-08) — REPORT_10 FIXES.** report_10: General / Full Invasion, prep 7:30, combat 1:04, 443 recruited / 428 lost, 142 kills, fleet 491, 299 buildings + 5 cities lost, 31 strikes, 5 AMI batteries, 105k IC banked, 1,432 influence spent on 0 research (every building costs `infCostOf = ic×.035+2` influence — that is where it went), no "Allied Units Delivered" row → no package was ever hired despite all five allied support buildings + Military Base + Heavy Port being built. My read (allies gated by INFLUENCE, not the ×100 IC; the 3× fleet deleting a normal 400-unit army) was OVERRULED by the designer: "no??? this is fine we just lost because i played badly" and "it's fine right now. it's supposed to be relatively difficult" — allies pricing and fleet mass stay. The real list, in their words: (1) "when i say guided missiles i mean automatic strikes" — every auto silo fired at `bestClusterX()` = the same cluster → claim ledger `G._strikeClaims`, `bestClusterTargets(n)`, auto/ARM-ALL salvos spread across clusters; (2) "what happened to strikes accelerating? they're super slow and also turn really slow" — `stratmis` was still `maxspd 540 / thrust 360` from 4.0.0 while the 4.1 pass sped every ordinary missile up ×1.4 → `thrust 900, maxspd 1400, burnT 6`, high turn while burning, low while coasting, `strikeEta` re-derived; (3) "a single [enemy] point defense laser can instantly delete a [strategic] missile which is not what i wanted. the regular system is fine" — the four enemy PD scans did `p.life = 0`; strat missiles now carry `STRAT_MSL_HP = 90`, each lance does `PD_LANCE_DMG = 30` (three lances to kill), ordinary player rockets keep the one-lance kill; (4) "still way out of order in terms of enemy waves" + "wtf happened to the bombadiers i never saw them last match" — first `doWave` landed at 6s during Phase 1 and the bombarder wall came 20s after the skyfall (≈78s, after the match ended) → no waves before Phase 3, supersPlan entries < 50s pushed past the phase-3 open, the bombarder wall (escorts + grid) fires AT the phase-3 open (50s), skyfall 8s later, first landing ~6s after that, carrier 25s into phase 4; (5) FRONT marker removed ("i didn't need a 'frontline' marker"). **Naming note:** the designer's "guided missiles" = auto strikes and "point defense" = the enemy's PD against strategic missiles — restate before building, always. **Built & verified** (`bestClusterTargets`, `G._strikeClaims` 7s/260px, `pickSpreadTarget`; `STRATMIS_BURN_TURN 3.2`, `STRATMIS_COAST_TURN .8`; `pdLanceHit()`; `buildSupersPlan()`; `doWave` gated `invPhase>=3`): 4 auto silos vs 3 clusters → 3 distinct clusters hit; strike >1000 px/s at 1.06s, ETA 2.90 vs measured 2.94s; strat missile hp 90→60→30→0 (3 lances), rocket dies in 1; phase-3 banner + first bombarder both at 50.05s, skyfall 58.1, first landing 64.1, supersPlan [64,60,90], carrier 75.0; tutquick/v403/v401/v41 green, 0 pageerrors.
