## 🦸 SUPERHUMAN / INFLUENCE REBALANCE — designer-specced (2026-08-19), NOT built yet  [SUPERSEDED — shipped above]
From the playtest loss (58 Superhuman Cadres → ran out of influence → couldn't build anything → sat frozen 280s of prep → orbital units shredded a frozen base). **Designer corrections (I got the framing wrong first):** the −2 influence/s per cadre is NOT hidden; base influence is FINE (do NOT bump it); the real problem is **~5 cadres completely zero out your influence and lock you out of building.** CHANGES TO MAKE (branch, still queued):
- **REMOVE the −2 influence/s per superhuman entirely** (title.html ~L4427, drop the `- 2 * (G.superN||0)` term). Move the cost OFF influence (which hard-locks building) onto stability (recoverable).
- **Stability cost: −1 cap/each → −3 (−3%) per superhuman** (L4397, `- G.superN` → `- 3 * G.superN`; keep clamp 20). Designer: "i never saw the stability actually go down" → make it VISIBLE (a HUD readout of superhuman upkeep). NOTE (Claude flag): at −3 each, ~30 cadres floors stability — a soft elite-cap by design; fine given they'll be buffed so you field fewer.
- **Let superhumans target GROUND** (L2644 `superhuman` def: remove `airOnly:true` so `airFilter` returns null = engages ground+air) **and INTERCEPT** (add `pd:true` so they call `acquireIncoming` on busters/rods). Update the `ds` text (it still says "2 influence/s and lowers max STABILITY 1%").
- **★ BIG BUG — fighters don't intercept incoming bunker-busters.** "massive screen of fighter jets but none targeted/attacking [the busters]." Air units only call `acquireIncoming` when `d.pd` AND they have NO other target (updUnits ~L5286: `if (!tgt && d.pd) tgt = acquireIncoming(...)`), and multirole Falcons lack `pd` entirely. FIX: give fighters/interceptors `pd`, and make air interceptors PRIORITIZE incoming high-threat ordnance (buster/krod) in reach rather than only as an idle fallback — so the air screen actually defends against the orbital bombardment. Balance-sensitive; verify a fighter engages a buster in a test.

## ✅ AIR EVASION + ORBITAL-LAYER FINDINGS — SHIPPED v3.83.1 to main (2026-08-19)
Playtest (7-city FULL INVASION, unpatched build): all 7 cities overrun in 94s. Designer corrected my analysis — the loss was NOT fixable by "build ground defense": buildings cost INFLUENCE, and the 42 superhuman cadres drained influence to ~213 peak → LOCKED OUT of placing buildings, AND unit army-cap was hit. So 57k IC sat unspendable (IC doesn't place buildings/units). v3.83.0 (now live) fixes the influence lock. Also flagged: the Autodrone Bombarder's PD shreds MASSED player air near a bombarded city (working-as-designed anti-air-spam); satellites need anti-orbital fire.
**Findings (verified in code):** Aegis Battery ceil .60 = ATMOSPHERIC intercept, NOT orbit — by design; do NOT change. Orbit-reachers (ceil 0): Anti-Missile Interceptor (ordnance only, not satellites), Orbital Gun / Particle Cannon / Mass Driver / Railgun Lance / railgun ships — these AUTO-target orbital enemies already (acquireEnemy), which is why "component defenses wipe the orbital stuff" works. So NO "auto anti-orbital net" fix needed.
**The one real bug — FIXED v3.83.1:** low-ceiling air units loitered passively and died to orbital railguns/rods they couldn't hit back. Now `hurt()` sets `evadeT=2` on any airborne friendly hit; the air no-reachable-target branch in updUnits jinks + dives for the deck + runs to nearest friendly base while evadeT>0, resuming patrol after. Verified: dives ~280px, jinks, then climbs back; still engages when a reachable target exists.

Do NOT rebuild it from scratch; it exists.

## 🏛 THE PROJECTS SYSTEM — the keystone (designer, 2026-08-19) ★★★ [PARKED — see banner above; design preserved]

**✅ v1 FRAMEWORK SHIPPED v3.83.0 (verified: 0 pageerrors, invasion intact, tab toggles, fog hidden→hinted→revealed, commit→build→effect-fires-once).** THE API (designer/next-model edits `PROJECTS` to add projects — all in `title.html`, defined right after `society()` ~L2878):
- **`PROJECTS` array.** Each: `{ id, kind:'forge'|'edifice'|'doctrine', name, branch, research:[...keys], teaser (shown HINTED), desc (shown REVEALED/DONE — the hidden payoff), reqs:[...], build:{secs, ic, speedBy}, effect: g=>{...} }`.
- **`reqs` condition types** (add more in `projReqProgress()`): `{type:'build',key,n}` (≥n live buildings of key), `{type:'unit',key,n}`, `{type:'ic'|'mp'|'antimatter',n}` (≥n banked), `{type:'research',key}`. Optional `label` overrides the auto-label. **LOCAL variant (v3.83.0+): `{type:'build',key,n,near:'capital',radius:650}`** → counts only buildings within `radius` px of the anchor city (the "capstone of an industrial complex"). No `radius` = global count (unchanged).
- **`build`**: `secs` (base build-phase seconds), `ic` (drawn down during the phase, research-style — 0 = free), `speedBy` (a building key OR object `{key,near,radius}` for LOCAL speed-up; each supporting facility adds +0.15× speed). Effect fires ONCE when progress≥secs AND ic fully paid.
- **★ MULTI-STAGE PLANS (v3.83.0+):** a project may define `stages:[ {name, note, reqs:[...], build:{...}}, ... ]` instead of flat reqs/build. Stages run IN ORDER — the plan HOLDS at each stage until its reqs are met (player builds the next tranche of infrastructure), then that stage's timed/IC build runs, then advances; the LAST stage's completion fires `effect`. Flat projects = one implicit stage (backward-compat). `G.projects[id].stage` tracks position. Helpers: `projStages(p)`, `projCurStage(p)`, `projAnchor(near)`, `countBuildingsNear(key,ax,radius)`. UI shows "STAGE n/N — name" + the stage `note` (narration — designer writes) + unmet-reqs, and a pre-commit "◈ PLAN: A → B → C" roadmap.
- **★ PFX EFFECT LIBRARY (v3.83.0+) — makes authoring payoffs one line** (defined before PROJECTS): `PFX.econ(mult)`, `PFX.mod(field,amt)` (any `g.mult` field), `PFX.society(kind)`, `PFX.spawn(key,n,near)`, `PFX.rule(flag,val)` (sets `G.projectRules[flag]` — the DOCTRINE global-rule namespace; consumers wired per-project later), `PFX.stabCap(delta)` (`g.mult.stab`), `PFX.all(...fns)`. Compose e.g. `effect: PFX.all(PFX.econ(2), PFX.society('machine'))`. `deepcore` is now a 3-stage local demo (Sink the Shaft → Breach the Mantle [real 8000-IC spend] → Ignition).
- **Fog** (`projFog(p)`): hidden (research prereqs unmet → rolled into "N undiscovered down [branch]") → hinted (on path, reqs unmet: codename+teaser+req-checklist, NO desc) → revealed (all reqs met: +desc +COMMIT button) → building (committed, progress bar) → done. **The designer-chosen fog model.**
- **State:** `G.projects = { id: {committed, progress, icPaid, built} }` (via `projState(id)`); advanced by `updateProjects(dt)` (wired into `updateGame`); `commitProject(id)` (revealed→committed). **UI:** `switchResTab('research'|'projects')`, `buildProjects()` (cards grouped by kind, live-refreshed via `tickResearchUI`). CSS: `.projCard/.projReq/.projBar` etc.
- **3 EXAMPLE PROJECTS (placeholders — designer REPLACES/EXTENDS):** `deepcore` (edifice, no-research, effect `g.mult.econ+=2.0` — NOTE: its 15000-IC req is a "have" gate, NOT a spend; set `build.ic` high to make it a real SINK per designer intent), `totalwar` (doctrine, no-research, `g.mult.econ+=.6; g.mult.mp+=.6; g.mult.capb+=20; society('dystopia')`), `grandarsenal` (forge, `research:['fusion']` → demonstrates fog; effect spawns a `titan` as placeholder for the future boss-scale unit). **NEXT: designer names/writes the first REAL projects into the array; boss-scale Forge units get their own polish passes (with the shared destructible-subsystem framework).**

**★ DESIGNER EXECUTION RULES (2026-08-19c — the build must honor these):**
- **DESIGNER handles EXACT EXECUTIONS** (which projects, precise effects, names, prose, balance). Claude builds the FRAMEWORK + extensible data model; designer fills it. "your ideas are good but i should handle more exact executions."
- **Requirements = PHYSICALLY BUILDING INFRASTRUCTURE with dedicated effort — NOT just IC/MP.** Multi-step plans / conditions you satisfy by PLAYING (have X Space Centers, build orbital platforms, etc.), and conditions you can SPEED UP or SLOW DOWN (more supporting facilities → faster build phase). This IS the "capstone of an industrial complex" made the core gating mechanic.
- **Not all projects are equal — some reasonably doable MID-GAME if you're bold**, not all endgame. Need a RANGE of scales/costs.
- **If 30 is easy, make MORE.** Treat ~30 as a floor, not a ceiling.
- **When we build the actual super-units: POLISH THEM HARD** — extremely powerful, fun to use, unique, detailed. (Deferred to the per-project passes, WITH the designer.)
- **North star: "let the player choose their sci fi, and let that physically shape the world beyond measure."**

**Designer's concept, and I (Claude) think it's the keystone the whole megaproject / bottom-tree / transfiguration direction was missing.** A **PROJECT tab INSIDE the research menu**, toggled against the research tree. Projects = super-massive individual undertakings; their output = **super-units at autodrone-bombardier tier and ABOVE** (stronger than anything you can normally field), gated behind **multi-stage event chains and/or MASSIVE stability/society upheaval**.

**THE UNIFYING INSIGHT (ties the whole psionics/tree conversation together): Projects are where each TREE'S CLIMAX lives.** We spent this session deciding the three trees have three summits — **top (automation) = the fleet/megastructure · middle (defense/energy) = the fortress-state / "they cannot break you" (its blurriest identity — sharpen toward ENDURANCE) · bottom (psi/bio) = the TRANSFIGURATION (posthuman shift, "everyone becomes nanobot shapeshifters," reusing the era-upgrade system that already re-arms your whole line + shapeshifter all-unit regen).** Projects are the delivery mechanism for all three. So: **Research = the doctrine ladder (tech you KNOW); Projects = the three summits (monuments you COMMIT your society to).** The in-menu toggle makes that split legible.

**WHY GATING ON EVENT-CHAINS + SOCIETY UPHEAVAL IS THE GOOD DESIGN — it fixes 3 old flaws at once:**
1. **Strategic resources finally matter** — a Project REQUIRING a stockpile of antimatter/rare-earth/uranium is the meaningful sink they never had (designer's listed flaw #6).
2. **Society stops being a sky tint** — a Project that FLIPS you to dystopia/machine/verdant as its cost makes society load-bearing (flaw: "society is a colour change + one modifier").
3. **The player gets a screen-scale answer to the enemy bosses** — autofleet has Skyfortress/Leviathan/Annihilator/Bombarder; player has only "big tank" Titan/Colossus. A Project-tier super-unit earned through an arc is the player's boss-piece. Symmetry.

**THE REAL LEAP — EVENTS THAT MAKE YOU ACT IN-GAME (designer, meatiest part + the biggest engine dependency):** turn events from "modal with instant `effect`" into a persistent condition you resolve THROUGH PLAY, and/or multi-option (not forced auto-resolve). e.g. a Project stage = "MOBILIZE THE INTERIOR — hold 3 inland cities under martial law 120s" or "you chose clean-antimatter — drop ONE tungsten rod before the arc completes and it collapses." This is where the conduct-reactive autofleet plugs in, and it's what makes the bottom "story tree" feel like PLAY not reading. **Also wanted: an INDEX / codex (browsable list of lore/descriptions) — cheap, high-flavor, and the natural home for the designer's PROSE.**

**DIVISION OF LABOR (designer settled this emotionally, 2026-08-19 — DO NOT offload the writing):** the designer WRITES (every project description, event chain + branches, codex entry, all prose + all choices — this is the part that makes it THEIR game, the whole point of doing it); **Claude/Fable BUILDS** (Project tab UI, the stage/gate/requirement/build state machine, the event-engine extensions [persistent events, multi-option, in-game objectives], the codex shell). The DATA (what a stage requires/does) is co-designed; the WORDS are the designer's, always. Claude may throw draft options to write AGAINST, never author the voice.

**3 HONEST FLAGS (Claude):** (1) v4-scale — build the FRAME + ONE project end-to-end as a proving slice (2–3 stage chain the designer writes, one resource/society cost, one super-unit out), NOT a catalog up front. (2) A super-unit stronger than everything can't be a WIN BUTTON — society/stability upheaval is half the cost; the other half is the TENET: finishing a planet-cracker-tier Project must make the autofleet ESCALATE (ties to the conduct-reactive/escalation hooks), else optimal play = "rush Project, win." (3) Place Projects ABOVE existing superweapons cleanly — existing = "research unlocks a repeatable strong unit"; Projects = "a national undertaking with an arc producing something above that tier." Some existing superweapons may get PROMOTED into Projects.

**★ KEY REFRAME (designer, 2026-08-19b): the EVENT TREE IS OPTIONAL AT START.** "combining 2-3 of these, even without an event tree to go alongside it, would let the total-mobilization fantasy feel complete." → **Projects v1 = cost → build → effect, NO event engine required.** Stacking 2-3 projects is already the payoff; event chains are LATER enrichment. This shrinks the first slice from a v4 megasystem to "a tab + a handful of projects."

**★ PROJECTS ARE THE FINAL PUSH / APPLICATION-AT-SCALE OF A RESEARCH PATH, not a path themselves** (designer). Research = you LEARN the doctrine; the Project = you build the thing it was FOR. Research-path projects show their prereq; NO-RESEARCH projects exist too (see below).

**★ THE 3 KINDS OF PROJECT (Claude's taxonomy, designer to confirm) — the KIND dictates how it's built:**
- **① FORGE** — pour industry in, get a SUPER-UNIT / super-fleet out. Antimatter Dreadnought, super carriers / ultra-heavy ships, extraorbital combat ships, Project Orion vehicles, mass superhuman production. **★ DESIGNER: these super-units get COMPLETE REWORKS to AUTOFLEET-SUPERWEAPON SCALE — screen-scale, destructible SUBSYSTEMS (not HP sponges).** ⇒ **BUILD-ONCE SYNERGY: this is the SAME destructible-subsystem framework the ENEMY OVERHAUL (§ below) needs — build it once, serve BOTH player superweapons and enemy bosses (your boss vs their boss, dismantling subsystem by subsystem).** Consequence: Forge is NO LONGER "nearly free" (was "reuse existing Antimatter Dreadnought unit"); it now wants the superweapon framework first — so re-sequence Forge AFTER/with that framework.
- **② EDIFICE** — a GIGANTIC megastructure with a permanent global effect. Orbital Ring (orbital DENIAL — plugs the anti-bombardment hole), **Deep Core Drill (THE late-game IC SINK → income firehose)**, self-replicating factories (auto-production), massive data facilities / AGI cores, combat space stations. **★ DESIGNER: ABSOLUTELY GIGANTIC + REQUIRE MASSIVE EXISTING FACILITIES as prereqs — and those same facilities FEED the project's bonus.** ⇒ **"CAPSTONE OF AN INDUSTRIAL COMPLEX" pattern:** the supporting facilities both GATE and SCALE the project (overbuild the district → bigger payoff). Honest IC sink + feels earned + payoff scales with investment. **RULE to keep it un-fiddly: legible "these building types within radius X count toward this project" (coverage-zone style) so you SEE your complex powering it.**
- **③ DOCTRINE** — a COURSE OF ACTION, no structure, a sweeping RULE change; where society states finally MEAN something. Hivemind integration, mass integration & decentralization, complete technologicalization, **respawn/immortality ("curing death" — the war-haters-vs-a-respawn-button-and-Little-Dark-Age bit; peak on-theme, KEEP)**, "hundreds of nukes in orbit to deny their orbital reach." 
- **④ WORLD-RESHAPERS (PARKED — Tier 3, each its own multi-week engine build; do NOT let them block launch):** full underground cities, entirely new ground layers, complete map reshaping, city/ground texture swaps, flying/mechanized MOVABLE city platforms you build on, moon colonization (designer's own "insane step up"). Amazing, not v1; bolt on later as event centerpieces.

**★ NO-RESEARCH PROJECTS KEEP THE CONVENTIONAL PATH ALIVE** (designer has PLAYED researchless — heavy interceptor missiles + conventional defense, hard-but-real). The Project tab is NOT research-gated as a whole; conventional capstones (Deep Core Drill, orbital-nuke denial, underground cities, heavy conventional fleets) are open from turn one.

**★ LORE FIT (designer raised timeframe implausibility; resolved):** the space MESSAGE transmitted the PRINCIPLES — humanity APPLIES given knowledge, doesn't invent from scratch. Maps exactly onto the existing premise ("concepts pre-unlocked; hard part is exotic application") AND the research/project split: **Research = receiving/understanding the doctrine (fast, it was handed to you); Projects = the monstrous industrial effort to MANIFEST it at planetary scale (slow, gigantic — the real mobilization).** Not a retcon — fiction + mechanics reinforcing. On "curing death in 3-6 yrs is implausible": the COMPRESSION IS THE POINT (Manhattan-Project-×1000, desperation, no peacetime timelines) — no timeframe was ever fixed; leave it undefined and let the absurd speed be a flex. Designer's call, not settled hard.

**PROPOSED FIRST SLICE (Claude, revised for boss-scale Forge cost):** lead with **an EDIFICE (Deep Core Drill) + a DOCTRINE (orbital-nuke denial OR superhuman mass integration)** — both reuse buildings/gmod/rules, prove the tab + cost + facility-complex mechanic + society shift CHEAPLY, no event engine. First boss-scale FORGE (Antimatter Dreadnought) rides in with/just before the shared superweapon framework so it's not built twice. **AWAITING 2 DESIGNER FORKS: (a) confirm the Forge/Edifice/Doctrine split; (b) first Doctrine = orbital-nuke denial (no-research, conventional) or superhuman mass integration (proves society-shift wiring)?** ~half the project list is from the designer's OWN FICTION (canon depth to draw on).

**SEQUENCING when we build:** (Projects v1 needs NO event engine.) codex/index shell (cheap, where writing lands) → Project tab + Edifice + Doctrine slice → shared destructible-superweapon framework → boss-scale Forge → LATER: event-engine upgrade enriches all of it. Also see: THE TRANSCENDENCE ARC (§ below), SUPER EVENTS (§ below), THE DECISION & SOCIETY LAYER (§ below), THE ENEMY OVERHAUL (§ below — shares the superweapon framework) — Projects is the convergence point.

## 🎮 PLAYTEST WAVE 11 (2026-08-17, v3.78 build) — "insanely hard but fair," big feedback batch
Designer played a real match. **VERDICT: "insanely hard... fights feel fair but the autofleet's sheer power feels harsh... very good but wow."** = TENET WORKING (overwhelming, not cheating) — do NOT nerf difficulty (designer did NOT ask to). ✅ CONFIRMED WORKING: interceptors mostly blocked the busters (v3.76 fix landed!); got a "long-term consequences" super-event ("great and funny" — events engine paying off).
- **✅ SHIPPED v3.79.0 — 48-STATS COMPLETE + 🚨 CRITICAL INVASION-RESTORE.** The 48-stats agent found & fixed a SHOWSTOPPER I shipped in v3.78.0: the wholesale Lane-B take planted `//` comments INSIDE a ~800-char single-line prep→invasion statement → `//` runs to physical line-end → silently swallowed `G.fleetMax`/`G.fleet` + the ENTIRE wave/bombardment/buster-budget config. **v3.78.0 on main had a DEAD invasion (fleetMax 0, ZERO enemies spawn) — confirmed by test.** Fixed (`//`→`/* */`), verified invasion restored (fleetMax 164, enemies spawn). **⚠ LESSON (critical): the v3.78 boot check only tested BOOT, not the INVASION — always force prep→invasion and assert `G.fleetMax>0` + enemies spawn before shipping. And `git checkout <lane> -- title.html` wholesale is RISKY (bypasses merge review) — it's how the bug shipped. Also: this codebase's giant single-line statements make mid-line `//` catastrophic — agents must use `/* */` when commenting inside them.** 48 stats: all ~25 missing categories added (damageDealt/Taken, ordnanceIntercepted, biggestKillBurst, byDomain, capitalsBuilt, resourcesCaptured, peakArmyCap, cities lost/captured/retaken, minStability, peakIncomeIC, dangerousResearched, society, era, intent/difficulty/prepDuration, peakEnemies, totalRecruited/Lost) + display rows per page + in the download report. Was: re-add the ~25 categories Lane B omitted (damageDealt/Taken, ordnanceIntercepted, biggestKillBurst, byDomain, capitalsBuilt, resourcesCaptured, peakArmyCap, fleetStartMax, cities lost/captured/retaken, minStability, peakIncomeIC, peakUpkeep, dangerousResearched, society, era, intent/difficulty/prepDuration, peakEnemies, totalRecruited/Lost) ON TOP of the shipped v3.78 base (no double-count) + display rows per page. Designer: "lets go ahead and get 48."
- **✅ SHIPPED v3.80.0 — COMBAT-FEEL LANE (all three, one Sonnet worktree, verified 0 pageerrors + invasion spawns):**
  - **EXPLOSIONS SURVIVE THE BARRAGE (designer's saddest bug — FIXED):** root cause was the particle safety cap at ~L5947 (`if(G.parts.length>1100) G.parts.splice(0, len-1100)`) removing the OLDEST particles — under a barrage the flood of cheap smoke/muzzle/spark evicted the still-burning fire/embers of blasts that JUST went off. Fix = PRIORITY cull: new `HERO_PARTS={fire,ember,ring,flash,molten,chunk,deb,wreck,plasma,text}`; when over budget walk oldest-first dropping CHEAP kinds only, fall back to oldest-hero (old behavior) just if not enough cheap ones. Cap raised 1100→1300. O(n), no alloc, PERF-NEUTRAL — saves the fantasy. Verified: pushed 1500 smoke+20 fire, one tick → back to 1300 cap with all 20 fire surviving. (`fxHeavy()>600` still drops DEATH flourishes — separate & fine; the barrage-eats-explosions bug was the cap, not fxHeavy.)
  - **MISSILE CONTRAIL DIES WITH THE MOTOR (FIXED):** the smoke PARTICLES were already correctly gated on `p.lit` (v3.65 burnout works). Real leftover = the polyline `p.tr` streak persisted at full length + alpha after burnout. Fix: `p.trCap` snaps to 20 while lit, decays by `40*dt` toward 0 while `!p.lit`; trail trimmed to trCap; renderer fades trail alpha+radius ×.4 when `p.lit===false`. Lit behavior bit-for-bit unchanged. **NOTE for designer: v3.65 already shipped burnout — if they still saw endless trails they were on a STALE PWA cache (service worker); a hard refresh/reinstall picks up the fix.**
  - **AEGIS BATTERY CADENCE (FIXED):** `aegisbattery` `rate:.1` (~10 interceptors/s) = a flak hose, contradicting its "one smart guided round per target, not a spray" identity. Slowed to `rate:.45`. Safe because v3.76 made each round HIT-TO-KILL, so 1 round = 1 dead incoming — still shreds a salvo, just reads as the precision weapon it is. Only the building rate changed (cruiser `rate2` untouched).
- **✅ SHIPPED v3.81.0 — CRASH-DIAGNOSIS ENABLEMENT + DARK-SPOT (Sonnet worktree, verified 0 pageerrors + invasion + fab asserts):**
  - **DARK-SPOT FIXED:** in-game only, a flat deep-space `fillRect(0,0,W,H)` in `soc.sky[0]` (fallback `#01030c`) painted UNDER the zoom-scaled world (frame(), ~L6780). cz===1 = world covers it (invisible/unchanged); cz<1 = margins read as seamless space, no radial corner blob. Title screen radial backdrop untouched (gated `mode==='game'`). Verified via 3 screenshots (title unchanged, normal-zoom unchanged, zoomed-out seamless).
  - **MOBILE CRASH LOG:** new `#dbgFab` 🐞 chip (bottom-left, dim, 34px) opens `dbgToggle(true)` on tap with NO keyboard; flares red (`.err` pulse) on any `logErr`; toast now says "tap 🐞" not "Shift+Alt+D"; window `error` listener captures `e.error.stack` when available. Desktop Shift+Alt+D unchanged. **→ NEXT CRASH: designer taps 🐞, screenshots the red line = the real stack → then we fix the actual cause.** (Root cause of kick-to-menu still UNKNOWN — this just makes it capturable on a phone.)
  - **⚠ PRE-EXISTING BUG FLAGGED (not this task, left alone):** ~480ms race in intro-skip `finish()` — its `setTimeout` can re-show `#ui` after `startDeploy()` hid it if a menu button is clicked within ~480ms of Skip. Worth a ticket.
- **✅ RESOLVED (was open) — the two designer questions from playtest wave 11:** answers captured below; both now addressed by v3.81.0 enablement.
  - **CRASH:** "didn't even properly close the game, it just instantly sent me to the main menu screen." Analysis: `backToTitle()` is only wired to 2 buttons — the game NEVER programmatically bounces to title on error (frame() catches updateGame + render errors and CONTINUES). G.proj(1400)/G.parts(1300)/enemies(~90-100) are all capped, so runaway-array OOM is unlikely but a mobile webview memory-crash → tab reload → restarts at title fits the symptom. CAN'T fix blind. ROOT BLOCKER: the in-game debug console (`DBG`/`dbgToggle`, L6711+) is DESKTOP-ONLY (Shift+Alt+D, L6722) and `logErr`'s toast tells mobile users to "press Shift+Alt+D" — useless on a phone. FIX IN FLIGHT: make the console mobile-reachable (tappable error chip + a discoverable touch affordance) so the NEXT crash hands us the real stack.
  - **"SIDES BEING WEIRD":** clarified = "ui edges still have that weird dark spot when you zoom out fully." Root cause CONFIRMED: frame() paints a deep-space RADIAL bg (L6770, darkest at corners); drawWorld's sky only fills screen-x [0,W]; at `cam.z<1` the world scales down around center → uncovered MARGINS reveal the dark radial blob. FIX IN FLIGHT: cover the full canvas with seamless deep-space color in-game so zoomed-out margins read as continuous space (title-screen backdrop must stay unchanged). Requires before/after screenshots at min zoom.
  - Still open/ambiguous: air forces "looked like this" (screenshot = massive white speck swarm) — possibly just the fleet at scale; revisit if designer re-raises.

## 🎯 ANTI-MISSILE INTERCEPTOR CAN'T STOP BUSTERS — ✅ FIXED v3.76.0 (AMI round now HIT-TO-KILL: `updProj` ~L5807 `best.interceptable → best.hp=-1` one-shot; flakHit callsites untouched so CIWS/Aegis still can't one-shot a buster; verified 4 batteries kill 12/12 busters vs old ~4/12, buster survives 1 flak hit)
**Playtest (research now works, played a full round): "really hard to win without research; orbital penetrators rip through; the anti-missile batteries completely flub the main attack."** ROOT CAUSE: buster `hp:200` but AMI round `dmg:65` → **4 AMI hits to kill ONE buster**, and a battery holds `ammo:4` → **one battery = one buster killed**; a 26–60-buster barrage walks through. (Rods `krod` hp~16 already die to 1 hit — busters are the leak.) The 200 HP is INTENTIONAL (flak `~5` dmg can't swat busters — dedicated defense required), but the DEDICATED AMI can't kill them either = broken counter. **FIX (tenet-clean, fairness not a nerf): the `ami` interceptor is a true HIT-TO-KILL — one AMI round destroys a buster/penetrator regardless of its HP; flak/CIWS/Aegis UNCHANGED (still can't one-shot a buster, so you still must INVEST in AMI; enemy stays hard). Makes conventional AMI defense viable → research/shields become an ADVANTAGE not a survival tax.** Verify: buster dies to 1 AMI hit / survives 1 flak hit / a 4-battery net kills most of a 12-buster barrage. NOTE: per-intent buster budgets are recruit 0 / officer 26 / general 52 / apoc 60 — if still too hard after the counter fix, tune volume next.

## 🖱 RESEARCH CLICK BROKEN ON DESKTOP — ✅ FIXED v3.75.0 (setPointerCapture moved from pointerdown → pointermove-on-drag; real-mouse click now starts research, drag still pans, cancel still works — independently re-verified on merged build: click fusion → G.research.key='fusion')
**CONFIRMED via real-mouse Playwright repro:** clicking a `.rnode2` node does NOTHING on PC — `G.research` stays null, keep clicking = nothing. Root cause: `#resBoard` calls `bd.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId)` on POINTERDOWN (added in the A1 pan/zoom rewrite), which retargets the resulting `click` away from the node so the node's `el.onclick = ()=>startResearch(key)` NEVER fires. Not the `RC.moved` blocker (moved=false in repro). **FIX: capture the pointer only once a DRAG begins (in pointermove when movement>6px / pinch detected), NOT on pointerdown — so a plain click takes no capture and reaches the node's onclick; drags still capture so pan-outside works.** Verify: (1) real mouse click STARTS research, (2) drag still pans + no research, (3) click active node still cancels. Longstanding desktop bug — high priority (research is core).

## 🪖 SQUAD HOVER — ✅ FIXED v3.74.0 (squad body seat `bodyS`→`d.s` in drawSquadUnit L6072 + drawSquadCluster L6095; inf went from groundY-6 to groundY, verified feet flush via high-zoom shot)
**v3.71's GROUND_SEAT_FRAC 0.92→1.0 fixed SINGLE units but NOT squads — and infantry ARE squads, so they kept hovering 6px.** Root cause: `u.y = groundY(x) - d.s` (inf `d.s=9`), single-unit draw re-seats with `d.s` → flush; but the SQUAD body draw (`drawSquadUnit` ~L6072, `drawSquadCluster` ~L6095) re-seats with the small `bodyS` (inf `bodyS=3`) instead of `d.s` → bodies land at `groundY - (d.s - bodyS)` = `groundY - 6` → **6px hover** (bigger `d.s` units hover more). FIX: seat squad bodies with `d.s` not `bodyS` (keep `bodyS` as the draw SCALE). `gdy0` aggregate-bars anchor (~L6061) already used `d.s` correctly — that's why bars sat right while bodies floated. Verified via programmatic feet==groundY check + high-zoom screenshot.

## 👥 SQUAD LOD GLYPH FIX — ✅ SHIPPED v3.73.0 (cluster draws min(n,3) bodies not 1+pips; pips deleted; `SQUAD_LOD_ZOOM` 0.9→0.75; verified: forced-by-count LOD at zoom reads as little soldier groups, no squares)
**ROOT CAUSE of "squads look like one unit with squares above their head":** `drawSquadCluster` (the SQUAD LOD path, ~L6090) draws ONE representative soldier + a row of "strength pip" rectangles above the head; LOD triggers at `cam.z < SQUAD_LOD_ZOOM(0.9)` OR `liveSquadN > SQUAD_LOD_N(45)` — both COMMON in normal play, so the player almost never sees the real spread fireteam. The pips read as confusing floating squares, not a squad. **FIX (render-only, behavior-neutral):** rewrite `drawSquadCluster` to draw `min(n,3)` compact soldier bodies standing together (1 shared shadow, no per-body aim/healthbar — still the cheap path), DELETE the pips; lower `SQUAD_LOD_ZOOM 0.9→0.75` so the full fireteam survives further zoom-out; keep `SQUAD_LOD_N=45` perf cap. Verify w/ LOD + full-fireteam screenshots.

## 🛰 SATELLITE FIXES — ✅ SHIPPED v3.72.0 (`SPACE_STATION_Y` .56→.48*worldH upper thermosphere; rod-armed space units gated via `airFilter` weapon:'rod'→ground/reentry/low only; laser/missile sats unchanged)
**✅ v3.71.0 SHIPPED** (bombardment fairness LANE A + render fixes LANE B bundled: 3 decoys, PD ranks rods first, interceptable orbital plasma, infantry seated `GROUND_SEAT_FRAC 1.0`, squads legible `SQUAD_DRAW_CY_FRAC .15`). Now:

Space units are `domain:'space'` (satlaser/missilesat/rods/spacefrigate/ostrikefighter).
- **Tungsten Rods sat must NOT fire kinetic rods at other SPACE targets** — the `rods` unit ("Tungsten Rods Zeus", domain space, weapon 'rail') is a GROUND-bombardment weapon; guard its targeting so it engages ground/descending threats only, not space-vs-space. Check space-unit acquire logic.

## 🧠 THE TRANSCENDENCE ARC — combos become DOCTRINES, not units (designer design pass, 2026-08-17)

**This is the fix for v1's single biggest missed opportunity: "a combo only ever unlocked a unit — it never changed the world."** The designer wants every research tree "infinitely more impactful," converging on a post-human capstone. Banked in full so nothing evaporates. NOT yet approved for build — framework-first, content-incremental (see feasibility note).

**🔻 DESIGNER PIVOT (2026-08-17, same session):** designer pulled BACK from going all-in on the tree-deepening — rightly worried it's a whole second meta-layer on a game whose #1 complaint was "too complex to learn" (cost us the entire v3.35 tutorial to claw back). **DECISION: do NOT deepen all the research trees.** Instead — (a) make the BASE game feel stronger first, and (b) deliver civilization-scale change through **MEGASTRUCTURES revealed/activated via EVENT CHAINS**, not through tree nodes. This is a BETTER container for the best part of the arc: the compute-economy-whose-cost-is-fragility becomes LITERAL (the megastructure on the map IS the building the enemy decapitates), and it's one legible object + a story beat instead of a lattice of combos. Reuses the dormant Super Events engine (`EVENTS_LIVE=false`) — that engine was waiting for exactly this content. Caveat banked: megastructures RELOCATE the cost (mechanic cheap, event-chain WRITING is the same big lift) into the STORY domain the designer wants to own — good division of labor (agents build mechanics + wire events; designer writes beats at their own pace). **STATUS: Transcendence arc PARKED as "megastructure + event chain, someday." Near-term = base-strength work (see below).**

**➡ CHOSEN NEAR-TERM DIRECTION (designer picked, 2026-08-17): "the ENEMY feels threatening" — the autofleet threat pass.** Per the tenet this is the ONLY real difficulty lever. Standing problem: enemy trivially easy (800 fighters delete the invasion; "10% of tax income repels it"). **KEY REFRAME (designer's own prior steer + perf reality): scary = SMART + AGGRESSIVE, NOT numerous. The "scale fleet to 1000+" framing is DEAD** (phone can't sim thousands; designer wants ~equal numbers + smart enemies). Contained first slice (NOT v4.0 orbital-siege megasystem): (1) **ADAPTIVITY — highest leverage** — enemy reads your composition and drops the COUNTER (air→AA-fairing flak; turtle→bunker-busters + long-range city-sniping artillery; navy→orbital rods) → kills the spam-one-toy dominant strategy, forces response. (2) **First strike that bites + telegraphed** (even Probe feels apocalyptic in its window) AND fixes the fairness flip-side (opening volley currently UN-defendable → give it a telegraph + interceptable window so good play answers it — playtest item #2). (3) **Aggression not sponginess** — focus-fire + high-value targeting (airbases/super-units), bosses get destructible subsystems not fatter HP. SEQUENCING: land the two in-flight lanes (capital fortress + squad) FIRST, open the enemy lane on a clean file. **FRAMING (designer, 2026-08-17): "less a rework and more CONTINUING to work on it" — layer meaner behavior onto the EXISTING enemy systems (escalation v2, adaptive buster-director, opening bombarders) incrementally and TUNE against playtest; do NOT demolish + rebuild. Small increments, telegraphed + tunable, iterate. Iteration is the safety rail against "adaptive = feels like cheating."**

**The capstone — TRANSCENDENCE (the structurally strongest idea):** a late, expensive, ONE-WAY opt-in that DISSOLVES manpower and re-pegs your army cap to a COMPUTATION economy. It does NOT raise the manpower number — it rips the governor out. Army cap becomes `f(compute)` sourced from infrastructure (Comms Towers, Data Centers, quantum cores), with **no upper limit** → spam hundreds of units. THEME-PERFECT: you mobilized so hard you stopped being made of people; your only limit is silicon you can defend.
- **⚠ MANPOWER CONSTRAINT (do NOT violate):** designer said manpower is "perfect where it is right now" / "definitely don't want to change the way manpower works." Reconciliation Claude proposed and designer implicitly building on: base manpower stays 100% untouched for everyone; Transcendence is an opt-in doctrine to ESCAPE manpower, not a change to it. CONFIRM this reading before building — if designer means manpower literally persists under it, redesign.
- **The cost is FRAGILITY, not a cap (tenet-compliant).** No ceiling, but the "cap" LIVES IN BUILDINGS. Bomb the compute network (data centers/comms/cores) → ceiling collapses → units go dormant/offline/berserk. The more godlike you get, the more your whole power sits in a few high-value nodes the autofleet can decapitate. *"You're a god, but your brain is in a building, and they know where it is."* Spatial, attackable, dramatic — strictly better drama than the manpower number. Reuses upkeep mechanic (compute-upkeep = reskin).

**The FORK (the society/policy shift the game keeps almost having) — a real identity choice, both reach Transcendence flavored differently:**
- **ASCENSION (bio/psi — keep the humans):** your PEOPLE uploaded/linked. Units = extensions of living minds: adaptive, high-skill, but tied to stability/population; EW/EMP makes them PANIC (there's a mind in there). Society → transcendent verdant.
- **SINGULARITY (automation/AI — build the minds):** you MANUFACTURE intelligence. Fearless, endlessly copyable, but spoofable/hackable, no improvisation. Thematically loaded — the autofleet IS a rogue machine intelligence → *"are you becoming the thing you fight?"* Society → machine.

**The ladder up to it (designer's sketch, cleaned):**
- **Psi × Robotics = Neural Integration** → brain-chips / mechanical frames; remote-piloted frames (no pilot to lose) = first node of the mind-network.
- **Neural Integration × Bio = Genetic / Neural Engineering** → selective gene editing, swap bodies on the fly mid-battle, CONSUMPTIONLESS bodies (energy-only, drop food/water logistics → economic shift).
- **Applied Psionics × Neural Integration = Mechanical Integration / Remote Consciousness** → link one mind into a jet remotely; one consciousness, many bodies. **NOTE: this is mechanically the SQUAD SYSTEM (one brain, many hitboxes) — the squad code is literally the seed of this fantasy; at the high end the squad becomes a doctrine (your whole army = one mind wearing a hundred shells).**
- Human-conscious-integration vs AI-systems = the fork above; "entirely interconnected battlefield/technology" is the payoff both share.

**Two design constraints (keep the whole arc canon-clean & shippable):**
1. **Bio & psi are "just as powerful" — but pointed INWARD.** Canon (designer's own correction): nothing biological hurts a pure-machine autofleet. So bio's power = rewrite YOUR OWN forces (regen, dirt-cheap super-soldiers, swappable/consumptionless bodies), never "infect the enemy." Psi likewise (buffs, own-network control, remote-piloting). Every bit as strong — strong at transforming YOU, not damaging THEM.
2. **The mechanics are CHEAP; the CONTENT is the "SO much work."** Engine hooks already exist: `society()` repaints world + adds modifier (fork reuses it); army cap is a formula (swap `mp` term → `compute` term); upkeep exists (compute-upkeep reskin); combos exist as prereq lists (just need to fire a TRANSFORMATION not only unlock a unit); `mult`/`gmod` global-modifier system + building `gmod` can feed a `G.compute` pool. **RECOMMENDATION (Claude, strong): build the FRAMEWORK once — combo-fires-a-doctrine + compute economy + society fork + cap-swap capstone (~a weekend) — then pour content in incrementally. Do NOT deepen all 7 branches at once; make the psi→robotics→bio spine genuinely transformative as the PROOF, and if it lands it's the template for the rest.** That's how this ships instead of stalling.

## 💡 DESIGN BRAINSTORM — 2026-08-17 batch (designer ideas + Claude's takes; NOT built yet, banked for build)

**Recommended build order (Claude's call): 1) Scorched-earth event + strike-cost decouple (both high-impact, on existing engine), 2) Nanoforge rocket-interceptor, 3) massed-fighter behavior + support/ferry helis (tuning-heavy → designer at the controls).** Designer to pick which thread first.

1. **⭐ SCORCHED-EARTH EVENT (Claude's #1 pick — strongest in the batch).** Solves a real hole: captured cities + landed harvesters let the enemy leverage YOUR industry to snowball (city-capture `ct.capture/ct.held` + harvester "anchors & keeps producing"), and the player has no counter but retaking. Fix = a grim denial button. Shape: bury a **denial charge** ("dead hand" — nuclear or antimatter) under a chosen city IN ADVANCE (deliberate investment — you pick which cities get the switch). If that city falls to occupation, a **decision popup fires** (the agreed rare-major-beat use of popups): "[City] is lost — detonate the buried charges? SCORCH (annihilate occupiers + everything they'd capture, lose the city forever, big stability hit + fallout) / LET IT FALL (they take your factories)." Engine all exists: city capture, nuke/antimatter detonate fns, the EVENTS engine (WW3 already used it). HIGH confidence, HIGH impact, peak "total mobilization."
2. **⭐ STRIKE-COST DECOUPLE (Claude's #1b — clean, well-motivated).** Designer: "using stability [as strike cost] seems like a strange measurement." Right for MOST strikes. Fix = decouple by taboo: CONVENTIONAL strikes (Heavy Guided Missile, ballistic HE, kinetic lance, cluster) cost **IC + cooldown ONLY** (they're ordnance, not atrocity); reserve the **STABILITY cost for the genuinely unthinkable** (nuclear, antimatter, firestorm, Mind-Forge). Makes stability MEAN something again = the price of atrocity, not of artillery. STRAT table + stratStab/stratCost.
3. **⭐ NANOFORGE + AIRSHIP ROCKET-INTERCEPTOR (Claude's fave fleet piece; combines two designer ideas + resolves the "is Nanoforge redundant?" worry — answer: KEEP it, repurpose).** Nanoforge is the sky fleet's CARRIER (only capital ship that launches aircraft) — NOT redundant, just lacked a reason post-jet-ceiling-cap. New **airship-native rocket interceptor**: Me-163-Komet physics — huge thrust, reaches thermosphere+ (where jets can't go and the airships/high enemies live), SEVERELY low fuel (screams up, kills, must return to carrier fast). Launched by the Nanoforge. Gives the sky fleet air cover at its own altitude + a real carrier-positioning puzzle. Reuse the aircraft fuel system (AIR_FUEL_BURN) + high `ceil` + carrier RTB.
4. **HELICOPTERS — support + transport.** Current rotor roster is all combat (Kite/Kestrel/Mastodon). (a) **Support heli = flying AWACS/C2** — mobile fire-rate+range aura over the front (mobile Ammo Depot + Radar), reuses `aura`. Zero-micro, distinctive. (b) **Transport heli — Claude pushed BACK on manual load/unload (micro cuts against the no-babysit fantasy)**; the buildable version = **auto air-lift** that ferries idle rear troops to the fighting (like Rail Hub but crosses water/terrain rail can't). Keep it automatic.
5. **MASSED-FIGHTER BEHAVIOR (higher-value half of "units leverage advantages").** Half done already (independent targeting, AIR_STANDOFF kiting). The missing half: fighters **rally & hold near the front until a WING has gathered** (or a big wave lands), then commit TOGETHER — kills the "trickle to death," turns air into the coordinated hammer. Tuning-heavy → designer playtest.
6. **PARTISANS (b-side to the scorch event; keep MINIMAL — designer flagged "hard to make work," Claude agrees).** Occupied cities bleed a TRICKLE of friendly partisan militia that harass occupiers from inside — slows the enemy's use of the city + softens it for your counterattack, but CANNOT liberate alone (else capture stops mattering). Friction + flavor only. Reuses city auto-recruit + capture (ct.held/retake). Build TOGETHER with scorch (resistance fails → you make the terrible call).

## 💡 DESIGN BRAINSTORM — 2026-08-17 batch 2 (fleet/ground/psionics + lore; NOT committed — designer said "don't commit yet")

**Claude's calibrated ranking (criterion: fixes a STRUCTURAL problem vs adds a toy):** the ground/sea capital path + the electralumikinetic uranium-beam CARRY WEIGHT; Superhuman rebalance is a clean correction; Nanoforge/carrier is tidy-up; mind-frames are a someday.

- **⭐ GROUND / SEA CAPITAL-UNIT PATH (structurally important — the missing half of "fortress world firing upward").** The Sky Fortress branch gave the SKY a whole capital tier; ground+sea never got one, so vertical combat is lopsided and the old "ground feels weak, one tungsten rod deletes everything" complaint STILL stands. Give ground+sea their own screen-scale apex, VARIED (designer emphasis) — each a distinct ROLE not a reskin: **Land dreadnought/superheavy** (player's answer to the enemy Land Leviathan — rolling siege battleship), **Giant naval battleship** (long-range shore bombardment, out-ranges an orbital rod), **Super carrier w/ 8 fighter wings** (naval air projection = sea's Nanoforge), **Giant mech** (bruiser above Colossus/Titan), **Mobile command fortress** (a BUILDING ON TREADS — rolling C2 aura + garrison; most novel of the set). DISCIPLINE (Claude insists): real costs + a build home (ground "War Foundry" / naval "Drydock" ~ Sky Dock gates the airships) + upkeep + DISTINCT COUNTERS — bigger must mean DIFFERENT, not just more HP (else it's the tungsten-rod problem reversed). We already have Mammoth (superheavy tank, v3.50) + Colossus/Titan as seeds.
- **⭐ ELECTRALUMIKINETICS + URANIUM SUNBEAM (best genuinely-NEW mechanic — because it FIXES the dead-resource flaw).** New psionic-adjacent unit beside Superhumans/"reps". Setting: electralumikinetics warp light & electricity; key ability = ABSORB uranium to fire a giant plasma-laser in a magnetic field that ERASES CITY BLOCKS (designer: "basically what the AI first thought the Mind-Forge was"). Gives URANIUM a dramatic active USE (its "barely used strategic resource" flaw = on the game's own wrong-list). Mechanically a telegraphed CHARGE-UP AoE (kill-it-before-the-note-lands). Distinct from Mind-Forge STRIKE because this is a fielded UNIT that periodically does it, not a button. Two variants: grounded sunbeam-caster + flying skirmisher (loses eye-laser — Claude: give the flyer a LESSER beam so it's a role-swap not a pure downgrade). FLAG: the erase must point OUTWARD only — must NOT vaporize the player's own districts.
- **SUPERHUMAN ("rep" = Reactive Energy Projector) REBALANCE (correct + lore-clean).** Make Superhumans REQUIRE Deflector Shields research (canon: the reactive-energy layer IS a deflector — tech gate finally matches fiction). REMOVE the influence/s upkeep tick (invisible, unfeelable). Replace with a VISIBLE stability-cap hit. Designer wants **−3% max-stab per unit** (up from −1%). CLAUDE'S ONE DISAGREEMENT: −3% PER UNIT is a cliff (10 = −30% of your whole ceiling, 20 = half-collapse) — may be the intended "demigods have consequences," but it hard-walls the count; consider ~−2% or a SCALING curve (first few cheap, twentieth brutal). Needs PLAYTEST, not a guessed number. STAB-cap = a cost-not-a-cap = tenet-clean.
- **NANOFORGE / CARRIER cleanup (correct but minor — Claude MISREAD earlier; designer corrected).** Rocket-interceptors go on a dedicated CARRIER sky warship, NOT the Nanoforge. The real problem = IDENTITY: in an all-airship branch, "the Airship one" (Nanoforge, named for a process, no one-word role) is the weak link. Fix: every sky warship must read as a ROLE (carrier/battleship/gunship/escort/flagship). Give the Nanoforge a sharp role (support/repair/fabrication FLAGSHIP) or cut it. High-altitude ROCKET INTERCEPTORS (Me-163-Komet: huge thrust, thermosphere+ ceiling where jets can't go, SEVERELY low fuel → must return to carrier fast) launch from the CARRIER.
- **MIND-INTERFACED ROBOTIC FRAMES (Robotics × Applied Psionics) — SOMEDAY, designer said don't commit.** Pilot a war-frame with your mind. Worth keeping warm because combos in this game only ever unlock a UNIT, never a whole IDEA — a real branch FUSION is exactly the missed-opportunity we flagged. Should be a proper combo node eventually.
- **Designer wants to write a post about Total Mobilization for the Claude community server** — sending Claude the draft. (Warm partnership beat; offered notes if wanted.)

## 🚀 THE BIG VISION — "SKY / SEA / SPACE BATTLESHIPS" (the next major build; likely Fable's)

The unifying insight: **the war is VERTICAL. The enemy is UP (orbital siege), not to the side.** So the fantasy isn't ground invasion — it's a **fortress world of capital ships firing upward.** This single reframe fixes the old "ground units feel wonky on one axis" complaint.

- **KEYSTONE — Deflector Shields → auto-shields on every unit bigger than a jet.** Researching Deflector Shields gives all large units (sea battleships, airships, spacecraft, big ground) a shield that **wears down instead of pops.** This *revives the battleship* ("one missile wears the shield" not "one missile kills it"), justifies the airship, and delivers durability all at once. Shields should carry **upkeep / power cost** (a capital investment you field a few of — the ONE place a soft cost is right, and it's a cost, not a cap).
- **Multi-weapon capital ships:** sea battleships, airships (flying AA dreadnoughts — power is compact in the air), and city-sized spacecraft all mount **mixed weapons firing at once** — railguns + lasers + missile cells. Apartment-block gunships with long-range plasma.
- **Weapon-swap upgrades:** planes carry autocannons AND missiles; research swaps autocannon→plasma and missiles→micromissiles per-slot. Reuses the existing `UPGRADES`/`effDef` plasma-re-arm machinery.
- Sea battleships explicitly wanted alongside sky/space — capital ships in all three theaters.

---

## 👾 THE ENEMY OVERHAUL — full design bank (build AFTER 4.0, but the design lives HERE so it's never lost)

**The fantasy:** an invader with orbital supremacy massing a VAST armada you can WATCH assemble in deep orbit during PREP — stationed ships, lit hangars, a countdown — before it descends. Build DREAD. **Phased doctrine:** First Strike (orbital saturation degrades your exposed force) → Suppression (bombardment + jammers ground your air) → Overwhelming simultaneous multi-front landings (burst waves, not a trickle).

- **THE CAPITAL FABRICATOR (the true boss & WIN TARGET):** a dreadnought the WIDTH OF THE SCREEN anchored deep in the exosphere, with **destructible subsystems** — dozens of hangars (endless fighters), AA batteries (PD that downs your aircraft AND rounds), missile launchers. You DISMANTLE it subsystem by subsystem, not chip an HP bar. Deploys **~40 passive laser satellites** the moment the invasion starts. *(Designer is drawing the hull art themselves — transcribe to canvas when it exists.)*
- **Counter-systems** (each hard-counters one dominant player strat; each has a counter-counter): **AA-Missile Fairing "Flak Barge"** (mass-dropped pods that pop into homing-AA batteries — punishes cheap-fighter spam) · **Orbital Laser-Interceptor Grid "Aegis-Net"** (sat grid that auto-lasers your missiles/strikes — punishes missile-only play) · **Orbital Rail-Lance "Skyhook"** (hypervelocity ground strikes, near-uninterceptable — answered by anti-orbital fire/shields/dispersal) · **Air-Superiority Swarm "Reaver wing"** (dogfighters that hunt YOUR fighters) · massed antimatter-slug whistlers + EW jammers.
- **More concepted weapons:** Antimatter Flash Cannon (telegraphed zone-vaporizing ray / kiloton airburst bomblets) · Scalable Plasma (drone-mounted up to building-sized capital plasma mortars) · Antimatter Flak Curtain (airburst WALL across flight lanes) · Smart Rod-Storm (rod saturation aimed at your densest cluster — punishes turtling) · Mag-Tether Grappler (yanks your sats/aircraft UP into a kill zone) · **Adaptive Fabricator** (scouts your composition, builds the counter) · Shutdown Pulse EMP (briefly disables a fraction of electronic units).
- **Flavor concepts (Claude's):** The Eclipse (a ship so vast it casts a moving SHADOW debuff) · Harvesters eat wreckage to fabricate on-site (punishes attrition) · Mimics (late-game copies of YOUR best units) · The Choir (synchronized antimatter emitters, "kill them before the note lands") · The Peeling Sky (temporarily strips your air ceiling).
- **Opening-bombardment arsenal still unbuilt:** nuclear atmospheric EMP · antimatter airbursts (shooting them down still detonates them) · hostile fighter swarms · microrocket drop pods · asteroids (~2500 HP — break them up or catastrophe) · **sun blocker** (deploys if the siege stalls; sits atop the exosphere; tanks stability; space-weapons-only) · centrifugal drone-flinger · constant light bursts · tungsten **cluster-munition buster** variant · flare decoys that actually BREAK missile lock · **relativistic planet-cracker** hard-loss (top intensity tiers only).
- **Bosses → fortresses, not sponges:** lower raw HP, real countermeasures (PD, spawning, hard-to-reach), screen-scale.
- **Scale & perf strategy:** fleet reads as 1000+, but simulate BATTLEGROUP entities (HOI4-style — one entity = a brigade with a count/HP pool, rendered as a cluster). Designer verdict: ~equal numbers + SMART enemies, not literal thousands (phones melt). The **mobilization preview / order-of-battle manifest** ("127 antimatter-slug drones · 339 air-dominance fighters…") sells the true scale in text — it also fixes "the invasion's scale and attack plan was never explained to the player." **Planetary approach view** (cinematic fleet-closing-on-Earth shot) = later dread-polish.
- **Enemy roster consolidation audit** (several ships too similar). **Escalation:** Invasion & Annihilation escalate if you keep surviving with the mothership alive.

## 😰 MORALE / ORGANIZATION — "shock" on the battlefield (designer concept, needs a design pass)
Make stability VISCERAL on the ground. A unit-level **morale/organization** state that mirrors the STABILITY score: when the nation is stable, units fight crisp and organized; when stability craters — because a city block just got leveled and a chunk of the defensive network crumbled — units on the ground **panic and enter SHOCK**, struggling to focus. Designer's framing: *"I'd be freaking the hell out and struggling to focus if I just watched all of Prague get erased off the map."*
- **Driven by stability + shock events:** baseline morale follows `effStab`; a catastrophe (city DISTRICT destroyed, a big defensive structure lost, a nuke nearby) fires a temporary SHOCK SPIKE — a jolt of panic that fades over seconds. Possibly localized (units near the catastrophe shaken hardest) + a global baseline.
- **What shock does:** shaken units fire slower, aim worse (lead/accuracy degrade — they were just given leading; panic undoes it), move hesitantly/scatter, maybe visually tremble. Recovers as stability climbs back and no new shocks land.
- **This is the GRADIENT the designer always wanted:** stability loss should "degrade your units," not instant-loss. The Capitulation Crisis (production freeze + ~30% mutiny at 0 stab) is the rock-bottom extreme; morale is the smooth ramp above it.
- **NO death spiral (critical):** because stability is now cap-based and RECOVERABLE (not a drain), morale recovers with it — a shaken army claws back, it doesn't spiral to zero with no counterplay (the exact thing the designer hated about the old drain). Keep a floor so low morale never = helpless.
- **IMPACT VARIES HEAVILY by unit + situation** (v2 detail):
  - **MECHANICAL units are immune** (drones, androids, mechs, autonomous, machine-family). **Organic/human units falter.** This is the strategic core: a robot army ignores morale entirely (the "machine" society answer).
  - **Faltering behaviors (a spectrum):** hesitate / can't pick a target for a moment · hunker down & get SUPPRESSED · full mental breakdown / lose composure · and at minimum become WASTEFUL — dump their whole magazine in a panic, accidentally over-fire missiles (burns ammo/upkeep, reads as desperation, not just "weaker").
  - **⭐ EVERY RESEARCH PATH is secretly a morale answer** (the insight that makes the tree cohere): Automation → immune robots · Superhumans → fearless demigods · Psionics → mental fortitude/conditioning · Biotech → engineered composure · Propaganda/defensive structures → SUPPRESS panic in a radius. This finally makes the research tree about the game's real question: *how does your civilization keep functioning while watching itself be annihilated?* — the "what does total mobilization turn you into" theme, made mechanical. (Answers the design doc's "societal shift was never represented.")
  - **Shield-watching stress (a PSYCHOLOGICAL input, NOT a shield nerf):** shields stay 100% mechanically identical — same HP/blocking/protection, zero functional downside. The ONLY effect is a MORALE stressor on ORGANIC units sheltering under a shield that's visibly taking hits/wavering (they're objectively safer but rattled watching it weaken). Flows through the normal immunity rules — mechanical units / supermen under a shield don't flinch. You'd still build every shield you can; this is pure dread-flavor on the morale ledger, never a reason to avoid shields.
  - **PRODUCTION shock:** factory/economy workers panic under catastrophe too (9/11 framing — "let me check if my family's okay"), slowing production. The gradient above the Capitulation Crisis's hard production-freeze.
  - **Ties into the DECISION/SOCIETY layer:** dystopian / "mean" doctrine choices interact with morale (a harsher society may suppress panic at a human cost, etc.).
- **UNIFIED PLAN — actual roster susceptibility** (from real `UNIT_DEFS`/`UNIT_FAM`):
  - **IMMUNE (no mind to break):** ALL buildings + emplacement/city guns · drones (`cdrone`, `kdrone`) · robots (`android`, `colossus`) · electronic/orbital platforms (`railgun`, `satlaser`, `missilesat`, `rods`, `spacefrigate`, `ostrikefighter`, `dreadnought`) · nano-constructs (`airship` "Nanofortress", `shifter` nanoswarm).
  - **RESISTANT (engineered/conditioned):** `superhuman` (fearless demigods) · `psitrooper` (psionic fortitude) · `cyborg` (part-machine) · `titan` (part-machine leviathan) · `shock` (gene-conditioned). `clone` = DESIGN CHOICE (eerily-calm bred-numb = resistant + creepy, OR expendable fodder = susceptible — Fable leans resistant/creepy).
  - **SUSCEPTIBLE (ordinary humans — FEEL every catastrophe):** conventional ground (`inf`, `scout`, `tank`, `heavytank`, `aa`, `arty`, `mlrs`, `sam`) · ALL naval crews (`destroyer`→`carrier`) · ALL air crews (`fighter`, `strikejet`, `pdjet`, `bomber`).
  - **⭐ THIS MAPS ONTO THE TREE:** start conventional = a fully susceptible army that feels the war → Automation = immune machine army → Superhumans/Psionics = fearless army → Biotech = conditioned. Your tech path literally decides how much your people feel the horror. That IS the "what does total mobilization turn you into" theme.
- **Fable's design contributions:** (a) high morale could give a small BONUS (crisp/disciplined = +accuracy), so stability is a positive lever not just downside-avoidance; (b) **Propaganda Bureau + Comms Tower should project a morale-STEADYING aura** (suppress panic in radius) — finally gives them a battlefield role beyond the stability cap; (c) dystopia/"mean" doctrine = suppress panic by FEAR (units don't break, but you pay in humanity/stability-cap).
- **BUILD IN LAYERS** (like garrison/stability were): L1 spine = per-organic-unit morale from `effStab` + shock spikes on catastrophe → combat faltering (mechanical immune) + clear feedback (shaken visual + HUD). L2 = research suppression counters. L3 = production shock + shield-stress + society ties.
- **Legibility is make-or-break:** the player MUST see why units falter — shaken-unit visual (tremble/hunker/scatter), a morale HUD readout, a "⚠ SHOCK — [district] LOST" jolt notification.
- OPEN Qs: global-baseline + local-spikes (Fable's vote) vs pure-local? how harsh at the bottom (Fable: sloppy/slow, never frozen)?

## 🎭 SUPER EVENTS (HOI4-style event popups — the delivery mechanism for the Decision/Society layer)
**Designer writes the events; Fable builds the engine.** Fire ONLY on major beats (never spam — see the popup rule). Each event schema:
- `trigger` — a game-state condition (first city lost · research combo completes · stability < X · invasion begins · super-unit groundfall · etc.). Earned, not random.
- `title` + `body` — flavor/voice (the designer's).
- `choices` (optional, 1–3): `{ label, effect }` — effect applies a consequence: ±stability, morale surge/shock, society shift (dystopia/verdant/machine), unlock/lose, doctrine change. Some events are pure DREAD BEATS (no choice, just land + fade).
- `once` (one-shot) vs repeatable.
The best events carry a real CHOICE that shifts what your society BECOMES at a genuine cost (that's the "what does total mobilization turn you into" theme, made interactive). Engine = trigger scan + popup UI (respecting the "major beats only" rule) + effect application. Independent of content — buildable in parallel while the designer writes. **STATUS: ✅ ENGINE + EVENT #1 SHIPPED (v3.35.0, on `main`).** Adding an event = push one `{id,title,body,once,trigger,choices}` object onto `EVENTS` (doc comment in-code shows how). `evtReqMet` gates choices on `{research}`/`{society}`/`{building}`/predicate. Next: the designer writes more choices/events; Fable wires the effects.

### 📜 EVENT #1 — "THE IMMINENT ARRIVAL OF THE AUTOFLEET" (designer-written; seeds the engine)
TRIGGER: late PREP (autofleet inbound, bunker-buster weapons confirmed). BODY (designer's, verbatim in-game): the autofleet is en route with bunker-busters designed to hit civilian housing deep underground; people are fleeing housing blocks into the wilderness, risking abduction & panic (housing blocks keep people fed + in real-time contact with family — losing them causes panic). CHOICES (only unlocked ones show — the tech-gate is the whole point):
- **Reassure them the bunkers are safe** (base): if many housing blocks are later destroyed → MASSIVE stability loss; cap unchanged.
- **Refuse to address it** (base): −~10% stability + reduced influence gain.
- **[Deflector Shields]** personal shield generators in housing blocks: all housing +1 IC/s upkeep, each gains a shield.
- **[Dystopia doctrine]** strict guidelines + prison for leaving + suppress info: lose stability MAX (cap).
- **[Transhumanism]** free data-link bionics (stay updated / call for help): lose a lot of IC, but much less stability/influence loss.
- **[Applied Psionics]** persuade them to stay: lose less cap than dystopia, but still some stability max.
- **[Robotics]** guard drones escort + grid-link the dispersed: like Transhumanism (IC cost, low stability loss).
- (designer will write more tree paths — see the tree→hook map Fable provided.)

### 🎓 TREE → RESPONSE HOOKS for Event #1 (CORRECTED framing — designer set Fable straight)
**THE AXIS (get this right):** this event is *the news breaking that the autofleet is coming and the population starting to panic.* Every choice answers ONE question — **how do you keep a frightened nation from falling apart?** The tech-gate is not flavor: **having a tech is what makes a particular promise BELIEVABLE.** Anyone can say "you're safe"; only a nation that built the shield dome can say it and be believed. Choices that are military actions (contest orbit, strike first) or empty threats do NOT belong here — an empty threat COSTS credibility (designer's point); those are a *different* event (a "wait vs pre-empt" military beat = Event #2 material). Coherent hooks, each a different honest-or-dishonest way to make a scared population believe it'll live:
- **Robotics** → "the machines fight, not your children" — credible only because you have the android army to point at (stab holds, doctrine → machine). *(designer already wrote this one)*
- **Deflector Shields** → "stay under the dome" — true because you built it (real durable calm). *(written)*
- **Dystopia** → arrest the panic-mongers, martial law — order through fear not credibility (stab up now, resentment/cap cost later). *(written)*
- **Nanotech** → "every city is a bunker by morning" — the tech makes an impossible shelter-promise real overnight.
- **Biotech** → steady the population (med/chem morale floor; faintly unsettling it's chemical).
- **Applied Psionics** → broadcast calm — the literal anti-panic tool (fits "every tree secretly manages composure" best). *(written)*
- **Colossus/Titans** → "look at what we built — *they* should be afraid" — confidence through visible overwhelming force (the population can SEE the giant).
- Fold plasma/antimatter/particle into ONE "defiance" option at most, or drop — belligerence reads as fear, not calm.

### 🧬 DANGEROUS-TECH EVENTS (the next vein — designer's idea: dramatize WHY each dangerous tech is dangerous)
**THE HOOK (already half-built):** every `dang:true` research already injects `sc` **unrest** into `stabOffset` on completion — today a SILENT number. Fire a completion event that turns that unrest into a *scene + a fork*: **double down** (full power, full unrest, slide toward a darker society) vs **restrain** (dial the tech back, less unrest, less payoff). This is where dystopia/verdant/machine get CHOSEN, not just tinted. Two chosen as the template (best setting + mechanics already wired): **Cloning** and **Superhumans**.

#### 📜 EVENT #2 (DRAFT) — CLONING / "How should we treat them" (designer-written, 1/2 — MORE COMING; do NOT ship until batch)
TRIGGER: on completing `cloning` research. BODY (designer's, verbatim draft): *"Human cloning. It turns out that unlimited budget, tossing restrictions on national security, state of the art laboratories, and the threat of annihilation can get things done. Despite this, the concerns for such a massive movement are very very real. There was already a major countermovement to human cloning, and they have only intensified as we've finalized our designs. There have been widespread protests at the potential application of these policies and (quite warranted) concerns and discussion around the rights of these clones. We have not made any sort of decision so far as to how exactly we plan to train these clones, the scale of integration or how we want to respond publicly. How should we treat them?"*
CHOICES (draft, 1/2):
- **Like humans.** — less stability loss but slower production.
- **Indoctrination within reason.** We need them prepared; a fundamentally different way to educate them. *(DEFAULT)*
- … (designer writing more)
MECH NOTES (Fable, tunable): the axis is stability/humanity ⇄ production/expendability. "Like humans" = smaller `stabOffset` unrest + a manpower/production throttle; "Indoctrination" = the neutral middle; the coming darker option = cheap/fast clones, bigger unrest, dystopia pull. Ties straight into the existing cloning +60% manpower payoff.

#### ☢ EASTER EGG (designer — DEFINITE BUILD, queued after current agents) — "WORLD WAR THREE / you win by losing"
Launch a NUCLEAR strike during PEACETIME (pre-invasion; naturally-spawning silos exist). Consequence: **instant −90% stability**, EVERY other missile silo salvos **3× nukes at your own cities + military targets**, WW3 begins. Then a joke event popup (designer's copy, verbatim): *"Well, you fired a nuclear bomb. The other powers retaliated through their own stockpile. Congratulations, the autofleet is now diverting course! You've won in stopping the autofleet! You just needed to turn humanity into a crater to do it!"* → a joke "VICTORY" ending (technically you stopped them). VERY BUILDABLE: the peacetime-nuke trigger ALREADY EXISTS (the `oops` achievement detects it), and the SUPER EVENTS engine is already built (dormant) — this popup can hook that. Small, self-contained, on-theme (the dark-comedy thesis of the whole game). Perfect first LIVE use of the events engine.

#### 📜 EVENT #3 (TODO) — SUPERHUMANS / "do the demigods obey you?" (designer to write)
Setting: flying near-immortal laser-demigods that ALREADY carry the game's upkeep (−1 stab cap + 2 influence/s each). Fork: worshipped saviors (verdant, but they answer to no one — bigger unrest/independence) vs a leashed asset (you keep control, they resent it). Mechanics already sitting there (upkeep, cap, verdant society).

## 🎖 MISSIONS + GARRISON v2 (designer ideas — need a one-word design pick)
- **GARRISON v2 = the city/factory passive-production idea (designer, self-flagged as overlapping garrison — Fable: it IS garrison, and that's the fix).** Extend the just-shipped CITY AUTO-RECRUIT so a PRODUCTION BUILDING near a city passively contributes ITS unit type to the city's defense (factory→tanks, airbase→interceptors, shipyard→naval), drawing on the garrison weight budget so it can't flood. Merges the two overlapping systems into one. CONTAINED follow-up, offered to build next. (Supersedes the older heavy supply-chain Garrison v2 sketch — this simpler framing is the designer's and it's better.)
- **MISSIONS (designer idea — forks 4 ways, awaiting pick):** (a) scenario objectives (real goals per scenario beyond survive); (b) ★ in-game SIDE-QUESTS (optional mid-game objectives → rewards; CHEAPEST, reuses the achievements + SUPER EVENTS engine — Fable's recommended start + gives the dormant events engine a real job); (c) challenge missions (hand-crafted hard scenarios + best-score); (d) full campaign (linked missions + story, ties to lore layer, biggest). Designer to pick a flavor.

## 🏙 THE MOBILIZATION ECONOMY (designer ideas — strong, on-theme; the game's title made mechanical)
- **★ CIVILIAN SECTORS / LOW-DENSITY HOUSING → WAR ECONOMY (designer's best idea here):** cities START with civilian sectors + low-density housing that consume space and don't help the war. You CONVERT them — raze suburbs for war factories, pack people into high-density war housing, conscript neighborhoods — visibly stripping civil liberties + swapping a peace economy for a war economy in real time. This is where MANPOWER comes from and where STABILITY gets SPENT. Gives the DYSTOPIA society shift a concrete trigger; POLLUTION falls out of it (war industry poisons the land — the "planet in shambles" tone). This is "total mobilization" made mechanical. HIGH PRIORITY design-with-designer.
- **MANPOWER CAP — keep it, make it LEGIBLE (answered the designer's "why is it limited?"):** IC = infinite industry; manpower = finite PEOPLE. The cap is load-bearing — it's WHY mechanical units (drones/clones/robots/superhumans, which DON'T count against army cap) matter, i.e. the engine that pushes you to transcend humanity (the game's thesis). Don't remove it. DO surface that it GROWS (housing/Cloning/Biotech/Titans raise it) the way the stability cap was made legible. Ties to civilian-sector conversion = where mobilized manpower comes from.
- **POLLUTION / SMALL TOWNS (flavor, part of the mobilization cost):** war industry pollutes (stability/health/land toll); small towns = smaller settlements for map variety + more to defend. Fold into the mobilization-economy theme.
- **PHASED / "DEFCON" ESCALATION (designer idea — Fable: cautious):** the tech tree + eras + dangerous-research gates ALREADY provide phased escalation; a second build-order progression risks REDUNDANCY. The GOOD version = an escalating PERMISSION ladder ("you may not use nukes until the threat crosses a line") — the "permission to do the unthinkable" theme the strikes gesture at — NOT a parallel build-gate. Explore the permission framing only.
- **HIDDEN RESEARCH INFO (designer called it "iffy" — Fable: push back):** hiding what research does fights the accessibility we just built (tutorial/Advisor/"too complex" complaint) and punishes new players. If wanted, make it an OPTIONAL "blind" toggle for veterans, NOT the default.

## 🗞 THE DECISION & SOCIETY LAYER (the "decision tab" concept — needs its own design pass with the designer)

The original game's biggest missed opportunity, per the designer: **research combos only ever unlocked a unit — they should trigger DECISION CHAINS and NEWS POPUPS with genuinely large impacts on the world and policy.** A decisions/events layer:
- Combo research (Transhumanism, Shapeshifters, Hive Mind territory) presents **choices** with world-scale consequences, announced via news popups — policy shifts, society transformation, not "+1 unit type".
- **Society states must become REAL:** dystopia/verdant/machine are currently just a sky tint + one stability modifier. The societal transformation implied by "total mobilization" should be visible and mechanical (what your civilization becomes).
- **Bioengineering is the designer's chosen axis** for society-level shifts (flagged repeatedly as the interesting tree for this).
- Ties into: Superhuman political costs, dangerous-research unrest, the coup/Capitulation-Crisis framing, and the stability-cap model.
